# Phase 5 report: atmosphere, climate, volatiles and erosion

Status: started 2026-10-31, after Phase 4F's close; 5A, climate on every body, passed the user's review on 2026-10-31 in five milestones; 5B, the volatiles, under way: the frost's seasons (F239), the cold traps (F240), the caps of the inventory's ices (F241) and the frost's own gas's (F242), the poles' shadows (F243), the sea's heat and its ice (F244), the seasons' air (F245), which revises 5A's cycle, the ices that come and go (F246), nitrogen frost's emissivity (F247) and the ground without its seasons' ice (F248).

Phase 5 gives the bodies their air, their weather's long average, their volatiles and the processes that wear a surface down: sunlight and the temperature it makes on every body, seas, caps and cold traps from the inventory, rivers, glaciers, wind, dust, sand and the chemistry that colours them. The plan (PLAN.md, Phase 5) runs it as seven sub-phases in order, each with its own gate and review: 5A climate on every body, 5B volatiles, 5C water and ice at work, 5D dust, sand and chemistry, 5E surface physics on every body, 5F Io and Thaumasia, 5G weather and clouds. Mars, Titan and Pluto are its test bodies for the look; an Earth-like and a tidally locked ocean world give the climate its numbers.

The order (2026-10-31), the user taking the recommendations: climate first, since every later stage reads temperature and wind, rather than building only what Mars needs and widening later; the weather model as the last sub-phase with its own gate, since nothing on the surface reads it. The materials grow by what processes make, olivine and evaporites added to the plan's list beside quartz sand (PLAN.md 4.10).

The findings continue Phase 4F's numbering from F234.

## The first milestone: the star's light on any body

5A begins with the sunlight itself, since every temperature after it is read from it. The ice stage knew a latitude's mean as S cos(lat) / pi, the equinox's day with no tilt, and nothing else knew more.

- **F234.** The year's sunlight at any point from one integral of the star's path across the sky: the day's mean through the seasons for a body that spins many times an orbit, the time integral over the repeat for one in an exact spin-orbit resonance, a moon's year and seasons its planet's. Checked against a brute-force integration: the Earth's equator 415.9 W/m² and poles 172.4, Mercury's hot longitudes 2.53 times its warm ones, a locked body's substellar hemisphere, the poles passing the equator above 54° of tilt (Pluto, Miranda).

How it went:

- **A moon's tilt.** A moon locked to its planet had no tilt (`rotation.effective_obliquity` was zero for every locked body), so Titan had no seasons; its tilt to the Sun is its planet's where its orbit lies in its planet's equator. `rotation.obliquity` is now that tilt for every body, and the presets set it: the Galilean moons 3.13°, Saturn's 26.73°, Miranda 97.77°, Charon 119.6°, the Moon its own 1.54°. Triton's orbit, retrograde at 157° to Neptune's equator, precesses in some seven centuries, so its tilt to the Sun swings between 5° and 51° and its year's sunlight is the mean over the swing.
- **Pins noticed.** Mercury's and Venus's presets pin `rotation.mode` free at their observed periods (their spins from tides are not modelled, principle 11); the insolation reads Mercury's 3:2 from the pinned period. Their spins from tides, a resonance's capture and Venus's atmospheric tides, go into 5A's list.
- **The renders.** Nothing reads the field yet: Mercury, Titan, Charon, Mars and Triton rendered at 1,024 px are byte-identical to milestone 140's in albedo, height, units and surface age.

## The second milestone: the energy balance

With the sunlight known, every cell's temperature follows from what it absorbs, what it radiates and what its atmosphere carries away.

- **F235.** The year's mean surface temperature from the energy balance of every cell of a coarse cubesphere (North 1975): the sunlight it absorbs and the body's heat against the grey atmosphere's outgoing radiation and the atmosphere's transport. An Earth-like at 1 AU stands at 287.8 K, 301 in its tropics and 257 at its poles; Mars 215.7, Venus 755, Titan 91.1. The temperature is exported as a map.

How it went:

- **The tropics ran away.** A first balance on the zero-dimensional model's radiative scheme found no steady Earth: its water vapour's optical depth, the square root of the column, made the outgoing radiation rise by only 0.31 W/m² per kelvin where Earth's zonal means show 2.1, and the tropics warmed without end. Water's bands saturate as they fill; a logarithm keeps Earth's zero-dimensional greenhouse and gives the measured slope (prototypes in Python before the engine's code, scratchpad ebm_proto*.py).
- **The poles too warm.** With a uniform albedo the Earth-like's poles stood at 270 K, 31 K under its equator where Earth's are some 45 K: the low sun and the clouds brighten the high latitudes (North and Coakley 1979). The albedo now rises as the sun's year-mean height falls, under an atmosphere by its column, and the poles stand at 257 K.
- **A floor that fought the balance.** Io's poles would not converge: a floor at the temperature its own heat flow radiates pushed back against the balance. The body's heat now enters the balance itself, and a cell the star never warms settles at that temperature by the physics.
- **Known limitations.** An airless body's mean is too warm until its days and nights (the next milestone); Titan's and Venus's contrasts wait on the wind field (5C).
- **The renders.** The Moon, Venus and Earth rendered at 1,024 px are byte-identical to milestone 140's in albedo, height, units and surface age, with the temperature map added. Mars moves as its zero-dimensional mean warms by 0.8 K (its albedo by more than 0.2 in log luminance on 0.1 percent of the map, its tallest shield from 25.4 to 26.1 km), and Titan at the level of single pixels, its water vapour's optical depth at 91 K changed in the ninth decimal.

## The third milestone: the day and the year

The energy balance gave every body the radiative balance with its year's mean sunlight, which an airless body never stands at: the Moon's equator came to 284 K, where Diviner finds noons near 390 K and nights near 95.

- **F236.** Every place runs a column of ground through the star's path, its regolith's thermal inertia from its grains in vacuum and the gas in its pores, the air's heat on its surface, the transport the year's balance found held steady: through the year until its seasons repeat and then through a day at each season, or through the orbits a resonant body repeats in. The Moon's equator now runs from 98 to 381 K, Mercury's hot longitudes reach 696 K, Europa's equator 79 to 124 K, all near the measured ranges.

How it went:

- **One column, two clocks.** A fast spinner's days are thousands to its year (Miranda's 21,700): its year runs on the day's mean sunlight and its days at 24 seasons from the ground the season left; a resonant body's day is its year, and it runs whole. The deep ground is settled at the surface's mean after each year, as it would settle after many.
- **The thermal inertia from the gas.** A regolith conducts through its grains' contacts and the gas in its pores (Presley and Christensen 1997): the Moon's 55 in vacuum, Mars's about 185 at 6 mbar, a bar's dry sand about 470, one process from the airless to the thick.
- **The Earth-like's seasons.** Without its ocean the Earth-like's seasons swing as land's do: its summer poles reach 295 K and its mean falls 2 K under its steady balance, still within the gate. Its ocean and sea ice come with 5B.
- **The renders.** The Moon, Mercury, Mars, Titan and Europa rendered at 1,024 px are byte-identical to the last renders in albedo, height, units and surface age; their temperature maps now carry the days and seasons, Mercury's with its two hot longitudes, and the finishing pass takes some 3 s more.

## The fourth milestone: the climate brought down to the ground

The climate stood on its coarse grid, a summit as warm as the plain under it.

- **F237.** Each place takes the climate's temperature by its own height and slope: the ground's radiated share follows its own sunlight and greenhouse, the rest the air over it. A slope takes the sunlight of the latitude it faces; a height cools by the air's share of the lapse rate and the radiation's share of the thinner greenhouse. An Earth-like's 3 km summit stands some 15 K under its sea level, Venus's 11 km summit near 650 K, and a slope on the airless Moon at the temperature of the latitude it faces.

How it went:

- **The greenhouse alone was too weak.** Thinning the grey greenhouse with height cooled an Earth-like's summit by only 2.3 K/km: water vapour's optical depth saturates (F235) and thins slowly. The cooling a mountain feels comes from the convecting air, which the ground follows by its exchange with it; the radiation's share of the exchange weighs the two.
- **One share for slopes and heights.** The same share, radiation against the air's exchange, decides how far a slope's own sunlight moves it: all the way on the airless Moon, almost not at all under Titan's air.
- **The renders.** Earth, Venus, the Moon, Mercury and Mars rendered at 1,024 px are byte-identical to the last renders in albedo, height, units and surface age; their temperature maps now carry the relief, an Earth-like's continents cooler than its sea, Venus's highlands near 655 K and its rift floors warm, Mars's basins and sunward walls a few kelvin warmer. Noticed: Venus's surface at the tests' preview quality holds one cell whose height is not finite (none at standard quality); the downscaling takes it at the datum, and its source is to be traced.

## The fifth milestone: the spin's history from its present state

The user's direction (2026-10-31): the present rotation is measured and should be the input, its history derived from it, how long a body took to lock and whether it could have; satellites included where they matter, a binary pair above all; anything that looks wrong flagged rather than forbidden.

- **F238.** The present period is the input and `rotation.mode` describes it; the history comes from the tides of the primary and the largest satellite, the satellite's orbit taking up what the spin loses. Earth's day runs back to 22.4 h 620 Myr ago and 4.9 h at its birth, the Moon at its Roche limit near Earth's age; Mercury reached its 3:2 0.81 Gyr after it formed; Pluto has been locked to Charon since its first Myr. No preset pins its rotation any more, and the plausibility rules flag what the history cannot reach.

How it went:

- **The lunar timescale problem.** Run back on Earth's present tidal Q, 12, the Moon reaches Earth 1.5 Gyr ago; Earth's oceans dissipate today some three times their long average (Bills and Ray 1999), and on 34 the Moon stands at its Roche limit near Earth's age, a calibration on Earth.
- **A lock run backward.** Pluto's spin, run back from today against Charon's tide, ran away to a 2 h day: going back in time a lock repels what it attracts. A spin locked to its satellite is held, like a spin locked to its primary, back to its capture.
- **Venus.** Its slow retrograde spin runs back to a 30 h retrograde one, the Sun's tide on it too weak to have locked it in its age; the thermal tides that hold it (Correia and Laskar 2001) want the atmosphere's heating profile, which the weather model will give (5G).
- **The plan.** 5A's last bullet, the spin from tides, became this; 'one temperature for every stage' moved to 5B, where the ice's sublimation is integrated through the days and seasons rather than taken at a mean.
- **The renders.** Mercury, the Moon, Pluto, Earth and Venus rendered at 1,024 px are byte-identical to the last renders in every map, the temperature's included: nothing reads the spin's history yet but the plausibility rules.

## 5A's close

The climate through the loop's history, 5A's last item, moved to 5B (the user, 2026-10-31): with the atmosphere held at today's state until 5B it would add only the faint young Sun, shifting every preset's thermal history a little, where beside the atmosphere's escape and outgassing it carries Mars's thick early atmosphere and an M dwarf's planets losing their water in its bright youth. The user asked whether the history would need every kind of star: the main sequence from 0.08 to 3 solar masses is modelled (F3), a low-mass star's bright contraction before it is added with 5B, and hot stars and stellar remnants stay outside the plan unless chosen. 5A's gate review follows.

### 5A passed

The user's verdict on the review page (2026-10-31): "Everything here looks good." The gate as reviewed: the Earth-like's mean 284.8 K, its Hadley edge 32.6°, Mars's mean 213.6 K, the Moon's equator by day and night, Mercury's hot and warm longitudes met; Venus's mean (753 K, 2.2 percent high) and Titan's on its relief (88.9 K against 93.7, its uplands far above its methane seas) near; the poles' permanent shadows left to 5B's cold traps.

## 5B, the volatiles

The order: the frost's seasons first, the atmosphere's main volatile condensing on the cold ground and holding it at its frost point, then the caps the inventory lays in the cold traps with their materials and their albedo, the shadows of the airless poles, sea ice and the locked ocean world, one temperature for every stage, and the climate through the loop's history with the atmosphere's escape and the volatiles' transport.

## The sixth milestone: the frost's seasons

5B's first milestone (the order above): the atmosphere's main condensable gas condensing on the cold ground, holding it at its frost point, and the pressure falling and rising with the caps.

- **F239.** Mars's carbon dioxide holds its winter poles at its frost point, 145 K, where they fell to 80 K; its seasonal caps reach some 50° of latitude in their winters, the northern 1,099 kg/m² at the pole by the end of its winter, about MOLA's metre; its pressure swings from 502 to 696 Pa, 28 percent, as Viking's did. Pluto's and Triton's nitrogen lays thin seasonal caps from their own gas. The frost is drawn over the ground at the snapshot's season, its slopes taking the frost of the places they face, and exported as `frost.exr`.

How it went:

- **The surface's balance.** The first frost took the step's whole shortfall, the column's conduction counted twice, and laid some 10,000 kg/m² at Mars's north pole; the column is now solved under a surface held at the point, and the surface layer's own balance is the frost it takes.
- **One atmosphere.** With every latitude drawing on a gas of its own, held at the body's pressure, Pluto and Triton froze over and their caps grew without end; the latitudes now run in step, the frost the cold ground takes the gas's own.
- **A step of 2.6 years.** Pluto's year is 248 of Earth's, its 96 steps each longer than its air takes to freeze out, and every latitude took the same air in the same step: its frost came to three times its gas. The frost point is now settled each step, at the one point at which the frost taken leaves the gas its pressure.
- **A pressure no gas can reach.** Raising the gas's mass until the year's mean pressure met the body's ran away where the frost's own balance holds the pressure: Pluto's frost reached hundreds of kg/m² and its air still stood under 0.4 Pa of its 1. The gas is now the frost's that comes and goes in the share of the body's pressure the air keeps, all of it on Mars and little on Pluto. Their pressures stand far under the measured, the frost radiating as a black body where nitrogen ice's emissivity is some 0.6: a shortfall reported, not tuned.
- **A locked Mars.** Mars locked to its star at 3 AU laid frost at 0 K on its substellar ground: the steady transport, held through the cycle, took more heat from it at aphelion than the frosted ground absorbed, and frost formed without the gas to form it. Frosted ground now takes the heat the air brings it but gives none, as ground its frost holds below its steady temperature would draw heat rather than shed it; the locked Mars lays its gas on its night side and poles. The diagnostic found the transport's local response, by Williams and Kasting's scaling with rotation, 30 times Pluto's radiation's slope.
- **The renders.** Mars, Pluto and Triton rendered at 2,048 px take their frost at the snapshot's season, the northern spring equinox: Mars's northern cap covers the ground to 50° north, frost on its pole-facing slopes past the edge, and its southern begins at 81° south; Pluto's northern cap reaches 63°, Triton's 61° north and 80° south. The caps' outlines follow their latitudes, broken only by the slopes: the cycle's ground is a latitude's, its albedo and inertia the same along it. The Moon is byte-identical to 5A's renders in every map; the Earth-like's and Titan's albedo and relief are identical and their temperatures within 0.04 K, their latitudes now running in step under gases whose frost points they never reach.

## The seventh milestone: the cold traps

- **F240.** Where each ice of the inventory would last: each runs its own column at every cell of the climate's grid, at its height's pressure and beside the bare ground about it. Mars's water goes to a pole, the north on ground at the datum and a deep southern basin on the model's own Mars; Earth's water to its poles; Pluto's methane to 20° north. A basin traps better than a height. Nothing is drawn yet: the caps that fill the traps come next.

How it went:

- **The Earth-like's equator.** Its water first found its coldest trap at the equator: the year's steady transport, held in the column, took 41 W/m² from the bright ice there as from its bare ground, whatever the ice's temperature. The ice now exchanges heat with the air over the bare ground beside it (F237's), and under the Earth-like's thick air it stands near the air's temperature; the water goes to its poles.
- **Six thousand columns.** Every cell's own column took 8 to 42 s a body, Triton's four tilts the longest; a fast spinner's climate is the same along a latitude, and its traps are run on its latitudes at seven heights, 0.1 to 0.6 s.
- **No residual carbon dioxide cap.** At the seasonal frost's albedo Mars's carbon dioxide ice lasts only in its deepest polar basins; its residual south cap, at 0.8, waits on the ice's own albedo.
- **Mars's water vapour.** The clean ice's albedo holds Mars's air at 2.4e-6 Pa of water vapour, where it holds some 0.1: the residual cap's ice is dusty.
- **The renders.** Mars, the Earth-like, Pluto, Triton and the Moon rendered at 2,048 px are byte-identical to the last milestone's in every map; the traps reach the sidecar only.

## The eighth milestone: the caps of the inventory's ices

- **F241.** Each ice of the inventory but the frost's own gas lays caps in its cold traps, from the coldest, its surface Nye's plastic profile from its margin, until they hold its inventory; a working liquid's only where its ice never melts, the rest left to the sea. Mars's water stands in caps at both poles, up to 4.4 km thick over 2.4 percent of the body, its polar basins filled; Pluto's and Triton's methane in domes near their equators.

How it went:

- **Each cell's own trap.** Read at each fine cell's own height, the traps let the relief of the craters decide: within a few degrees of a pole the latitude hardly changes the trap, and the ice covered the cells a crater's floor made colder one by one, a scatter of thin ice. A cap kilometres thick buries the relief under it: each place's trap is now its region's on the climate's grid, read smoothly between its cells, and the caps stand as domes.
- **Pluto's methane over the whole body.** Laid over every place, ice holds exactly its inventory, and a rounding decided whether the fewest places were sought at all: Pluto's methane lay 4 m thick over the whole body. Ice that may cover every place now spreads over all of them only if no fewer hold it.
- **No ice on the Earth-like.** Its summer poles stand at some 295 K, its seasons land's until its ocean holds them (5A), and its water's ice melts everywhere: it lays no ice sheets, and its sea keeps all its water.
- **The renders.** Mars, Pluto and Triton rendered at 2,048 px take their caps; the Earth-like, Titan and the Moon are byte-identical to the last renders in every map.

## The ninth milestone: the frost's own gas's caps

- **F242.** The frost's own gas lays its caps last, where its ice grows the most: Mars's carbon dioxide in a basin at 77 to 79° north, up to 0.9 km thick over its water's ice; Pluto's nitrogen mostly between 37 and 43° south, Triton's at both poles. Mars's preset holds its carbon dioxide as measured, 0.12 m of its liquid where it held 8 m of ice.

How it went:

- **Mars's carbon dioxide.** The preset held 8 m of carbon dioxide ice over the whole body: laid, it would have stood a hundred times Mars's residual cap and buried deposits. It holds 0.12 m now (Phillips et al. 2011).
- **North where Mars's is south.** The model's Mars's south polar band lies lower than its north, where Mars's stands 3 to 4 km higher, and its basins' air raises the frost's point: its carbon dioxide lies in a northern basin, where Mars's residual cap stands at its south pole at an albedo of some 0.8. 5B's gate wants it in the south.
- **Pluto's nitrogen.** At the pressure the frost holds, Pluto's nitrogen ice loses mass everywhere, and its caps lie where it loses the least, at 40° south; Pluto's gathered in Sputnik Planitia over its history, which comes with the volatiles' transport.
- **The air's reservoir.** The caps of the frost's own gas do not yet hold the air: their exchange with it waits on the volatiles' transport, where the reservoir's history matters.
- **The renders.** Mars, Pluto and Triton rendered at 2,048 px take their caps; the Earth-like and the Moon are byte-identical to the last renders in every map.

## The tenth milestone: the poles' shadows

- **F243.** Ground near an airless pole whose horizon, in every direction, stands above the highest the sun's disc climbs never sees it, and stands at what its walls and the body's heat hold it at: a crater at the Moon's south pole keeps its floor at 41 K; the Moon's polar craters keep shadowed crescents under their equatorward walls, 39 K at the coldest, Mercury's 45 K, Ceres's 29 K. 5A's gate item met only in part, the poles' permanent shadows, is met.

How it went:

- **The sun at a pole.** Near a pole the sun's azimuth is its hour angle, and at the solstice it stands highest toward the equator, at the colatitude and the tilt; a place is shadowed where its horizon in each direction stands over the sun's highest there, the disc's top included, and the equator's direction, where the sun stands highest, is tried first.
- **Too many shadows on the Moon.** Its shadowed share comes to some three times the measured: near the pole, where the sun climbs under 3°, a cell a few hundred metres lower than its neighbours is shadowed, and the finish's cells, some 5 km, do not resolve the smallest craters that hold most of the Moon's shadow.
- **The renders.** The Moon, Mercury, Ceres and Callisto rendered at 2,048 px take their shadows in their temperatures alone; Mars, tilted 25°, is byte-identical.

## The eleventh milestone: the sea's heat and its ice

- **F244.** A sea of water runs its own year, its 50 m mixed layer holding its seasons and freezing ice that grows and melts by Semtner's thermodynamics; the air over a place mixes its land's and its sea's. The Earth-like's sea at 45° swings 8 K where its land swings 80; its sea ice stands at the end of its northern winter from some 60° to the pole. The ice is exported and reported, not drawn: the climate's ice line near 60° leaves a belt of it there.

How it went:

- **Ice that never melted.** Its top held at the freezing point, as the frost's is, the ice grew some 10 m a winter and never melted; as Semtner's zero layers, its top cooled under its snow, and the air's heat and its melting ice's albedo let the summer take it back.
- **The land's air.** The ice's air at first was its land's, the continent's winter: the sea at 45° froze. The air over a place now mixes its land's and its sea's by their shares, and a sea that is most of its latitude keeps its own.
- **The climate's transport.** Laid on the ice's top, the transport radiated away and left the ice 10 to 13 m thick at 55° south, where the Southern Ocean stands open; through the sea's water, as a slab ocean's heat transport comes, it melts the ice's base. Where the climate's ice line stands, near 60°, the ice still keeps most of the year.
- **A belt of ice near 60°.** Drawn, the Earth-like's sea ice covered 14.9 percent of the body in March, four times Earth's: a belt near 60° in both hemispheres, around an open southern polar sea melted by its polar day, and the map's mean fell 2.1 K under the 5A gate's. The climate's year's balance there stands at 268 K, its ice line (F235); the sea's year follows it. A restoring toward that balance would only hold it frozen: the ice is exported, not drawn, and the map keeps the climate's temperatures, until the ice line stands where Earth's does.
- **A world frozen on average.** The locked ocean world, an Earth-like locked to an M dwarf at 0.0485 AU, takes 0.44 of Earth's sunlight and stands at 209.5 K on average, its water solid at its mean: it has no sea. Its sea waits on the working liquid read from the local climate, the next milestone.
- **The renders.** The Earth-like rendered at 2,048 px is byte-identical to the last renders in every map, its ice exported in sea_ice.exr alone; Mars and the Moon, without seas, likewise.

## The twelfth milestone: the seasons' air

- **F245.** 5A's cycle revised (the user, 2026-11-01): the places run in step under their air, which holds its column's heat, exchanges heat with its land and its sea by their shares and carries the transport between places, solved at every step, so that the transport answers the seasons; a sea's water carries a share of it. The Earth-like's mean through its seasons stands at its steady balance; its air swings as Earth's does, 27 K over 80° north and 10 K over 45°; its sea's ice comes and goes from 55 to 70° and lies all year near the poles; it is drawn, and the sea's cells take the sea's temperatures.

How it went:

- **The sea's ice kept 60°.** With the air answering the seasons, the Earth-like's mean held at 287.0 K and its air swung as Earth's does; but over a sea at every latitude its ice lay all year poleward of 65° and covered 11.2 percent of the body at the snapshot. The sea's water took the transport only through its air, and its ice kept the air's heat off it: so a slab ocean without an ocean's transport freezes, as an aquaplanet's does. Carried 30 percent of the transport through its water across the sea's share of each boundary, the ocean's share at 20 to 30° (Trenberth and Caron 2001), the ice withdrew to 75°, came and went from 55 to 70°, and the mean rose to 288.0 K.
- **The transport through thin air.** Williams and Kasting's coefficient lets a slowly turning thin atmosphere carry as much as Venus's: Mars locked to its star took the cap of ten times Earth's. Capped too at its column carried by the surface wind across the body, it collapses onto its night side, at 29 to 33 Pa, as thin air on a planet locked to its star does (Wordsworth 2015). No preset reaches that cap.
- **A resonant body's air.** Solved at the insolation's 720 steps an orbit, locked Mars's air took 75 of its cycle's 82 s, by conjugate gradients preconditioned along each latitude, some 300 steps a solve. Stepped at a fast spinner's year's steps an orbit, and preconditioned by the system's mean along each latitude, solved exactly by its longitudes' Fourier modes, a solve takes 15 steps and the cycle 23 s; a locked Earth-like's air, its own heat outweighing its transport's spread, takes one or two steps, its cycle under 2 s.
- **The frost's own gas's traps.** Under the cycle's air, which brings the frost its heat by the greenhouse's radiation too, the frost's own gas's ice loses mass at every trap: Mars's carbon dioxide some 315 kg/m² a year at its poles, Pluto's nitrogen everywhere. The caps still stand where it loses the least, Mars's carbon dioxide at its poles, Pluto's nitrogen at 40° south.
- **Ice sheets on a latitude.** With the summers the air now gives it, the Earth-like's polar land kept its ice, and its water laid caps, 17 m over 2.3 percent of the body; but their margins ran along a latitude, the traps' air the datum's at every height. The traps' air cooled at the free air's lapse rate turned Mars's basins into worse traps than its heights, against the frost point the basin's pressure raises: near its ground the air follows the ground, and Mars's barely cools with height. Cooled as the ground is (F237), the caps follow the Earth-like's highlands, 39.8 m over 3.9 percent of the body, and Mars's basins trap the better again.
- **The renders.** The Earth-like rendered at 2,048 px draws its sea's ice, 8.4 percent of the body at the snapshot, and its sea's temperatures, its mean 287.1 K, its water's caps 3.9 percent of the body where none lay before; Mars's mean through its seasons falls from 211.3 to 202.6 K, its equator's from 231.5 to 218.7 K, its pressure 507 to 694 Pa, its caps 2.5 percent of the body in water and 0.01 in carbon dioxide; Pluto's and Triton's pressures 0.005 to 0.026 Pa and 0.0004 to 0.073 Pa, Pluto's nitrogen caps in its southern mid-latitudes; Venus and Titan at their steady balances, 755.3 and 91.1 K.

## The thirteenth milestone: the ices that come and go

- **F246.** The user's dynamic caps (2026-11-01): an RGBA map of the ices that come and go through the year, R the phase each freezes at, G the phase it melts out at, B its thickness at its peak, A the phase of its peak, from which an engine or a film draws the caps at any season. Mars's carbon dioxide frost, the Earth-like's sea ice and Pluto's nitrogen frost come and go as their snapshots show them, the windows at the snapshot's phase matching the snapshot's maps on 98 to 99 percent of their pixels.

How it went:

- **The window.** An ice's year is a run of steps it lies through, round the year's end where a winter spans it; taken as the longest run, a mass past half its colour hidden counting as lying, as the drawn maps count it. Its phases are shares of the year's time from the northern spring equinox, so that an engine's clock reads them, and it lies all year as 0 and 1.
- **Phases between places.** A phase cannot be blended across the year's end: read between the cycle's places on the circle of the year, and nearest the cell when exported, while the thickness blends.
- **A marine pole's thick ice.** The Earth-like's north pole lies wholly under sea, and its ice, its top never melting under its column's radiation, thickens past its cycles, 9.8 m at its peak; over a sea that is 71 percent of every latitude it stands at 4.2 m, the land beside it warming the summer's air.
- **The renders.** The Earth-like and Mars rendered at 2,048 px are byte-identical to the last renders in every map they had, the cryosphere map written beside them, and Pluto with them: Mars's thickest 0.94 m, Pluto's 0.08 m, the Earth-like's 9.8 m.

## The fourteenth milestone: nitrogen frost's emissivity

- **F247.** The user's call (2026-11-01): nitrogen's frost radiates at its published emissivities, 0.3 in its alpha phase and 0.8 in its beta phase above 35.61 K. Pluto's pressure through its year climbs to 0.87 Pa, near the 1 Pa measured, where it stood under 0.03; Triton's to 1.20 Pa, near its 1.4.

How it went:

## The 5B gate check and its review page

Rendered at 2,048 px at 7a94bd4 (Mars, Pluto, Triton, TRAPPIST-1e and the Earth) and measured on the maps:

## The seventy-fourth milestone: the vapour's transport

- **F373.** The wind's drift across the isobars, the frictional layer's alone.
- **F374.** The drift carries the vapour its layer holds; the air along the isobars its column.
- **F375.** The eddies carry vapour as the latent share of their heat, beyond the cell.
- **F376.** The sea gives into its surface air, 0.8 of saturation.
- **F377.** The land sheds the share of its rain that falls on ground already full.
- **F378.** The water's state against GPCP, ERA5 and CERES; KNOWN.

How it went:

- **The diagnosis.** A by-latitude table of the stated year's water (a temporary test, 4 minutes a run) beside the measurements gathered for it (GPCP v2.3, ERA5, ICOADS, CERES by ten degrees; the research notes, "Rain evaporation and vapour transport by latitude"). The Earth-like rained 13.4 mm a day at its equator and 0.7 at 30 degrees (GPCP's zonal mean 6.1 at most and 1.9). The wind carried 7e9 kg/s of vapour toward the equator across 10 degrees, where Earth's net is 0.1 to 1.0e9 and its Hadley cells alone carry 0.8 to 1.3e9; the mixing threw two thirds of it back. The cause: each place's whole column rode its surface wind. On Earth the equatorward flow is the trades' frictional drift, and ERA5's Hadley cells carry 0.45 to 0.51 of what the whole column at the 10 m wind would.
- **The drift.** The wind's own slab already holds the answer: the mass its cross-isobar flow carries is the Ekman transport of its stress, whatever its depth. So the wind is split where it is made: the part along the isobars, which the air above shares, and the drift across them, the frictional layer's alone; the drift carries the vapour of its own 250 m, a tenth of the column. A first trial of that alone flattened the rain, 6.4 mm a day at the equator, 4.5 at 20 degrees and 3.3 at 30 (GPCP 1.9), 1.2 at 60 (2.3): the mixing, at its old strength, carried off what the drift gathered.
- **The eddies.** Mixed as a tracer through the whole air's mass, the vapour crossed 40 degrees at half Earth's rate once the drift no longer carried the column there; within the cell the same mixing undid the drift. Two published results set both right without a constant: the eddies carry vapour as the latent share of their heat (Frierson et al. 2007), which mixes the surface air's vapour through the air they exchange, the less air a kelvin the more latent heat it holds; and the eddies' share of the transport fades inside the overturning cell (Siler et al. 2018), here by the cell's own reach. Across 40 degrees the vapour's flux rose from 0.5 to 0.6e9 kg/s; ERA5's is 0.9 to 1.2e9 (KNOWN: the transport itself carries 4.6 PW there where Earth's some 5.6).
- **The sea's air.** The sea evaporated into air as dry as its column, 0.6 to 0.76 of saturation, where ICOADS's surface air stands at 0.79 over nine tenths of the oceans. At 0.8, the humidity the clouds and the column already read, its sea stands 0.91 K over its air (measured 0.6 to 1.6) where 0.2 to 0.7, and the body's rain fell from 3.58 to 3.11 mm a day.
- **The land's shedding.** With the rain spread as Earth's, the land's bucket shed 83 kg/m² a year: it sheds only what overflows it, and a year's smooth rain seldom does. The share of the rain that runs off now grows with the square of the bucket's fill, the share of its ground already full; with the bucket's evaporation that holds Budyko's curve within 0.05. The land sheds 200 kg/m² a year, and the body's rain stands at 2.98 mm a day (GPCP 2.69, ERA5 2.93).
- **By the measurements.** Rain by ten degrees from the equator: 6.15, 3.44, 2.52, 2.50, 2.78, 1.70, 1.10, 0.79 and 0.67 mm a day (GPCP 4.54, 2.61, 1.92, 2.56, 2.77, 2.75, 1.85, 0.83, 0.46). The clouds over the stated year keep 30.0 W/m² and take 51.4 (CERES 25.8 and 45.3), their net -21.4 where -27.6 (CERES -19.5); within ten degrees of the equator they keep 42 to 44 where 52 to 56 (CERES 32 to 38). The clear sky over the body sends 265.9 (CERES 266.1).
- **Under its own clouds.** The Earth-like under its own clouds stands at 284.7 K where 281.9, reflecting 0.330 where 0.339 (Earth's 0.29), its last years' means 285.01, 285.06, 284.97 and 285.10 K. Its polar ice still grows after 80 years, 37 m of it, its polar night's air 28.3 K over its ice's top (Earth's under 25) and its clouds there keeping -8.6 W/m². Its checks now hold it within 5 K of 288 where 12 and under 0.345 where 0.37, its polar night's clouds sending Earth's 25 to 60 W/m² down again; the settled ice, the albedo's 0.26 to 0.33 and the polar night's gap, clouds and clear sky are printed, to restore.
- **The other bodies.** TRAPPIST-1e: under the stated law's year its rain follows its open sea's evaporation, 5,900 kg/m² a year under its star where 9,400, and its night lies under low cloud over 0.60 of it that keeps 4 W/m² from space (THAI's four models, Sergeev et al. 2022: low cloud at 1 to 3 km over 15 to 70 percent of it, its clouds' effect there -5.0 to 0.3 W/m²), where its whole column carried off on its surface wind had left its night's air at a tenth of saturation and clear. M66's check, that its day's clouds stand over three times its night's, is now THAI's night: its towers and its anvils its day's alone, its night's cover within 0.1 to 0.75 and its clouds' effect there within 6 W/m². Under its own clouds its mean stands at 249.6 K where 250.9 (THAI 232 to 246), its dayside at 267.1 where 268.0 (261 to 268), its albedo 0.258 (0.22 to 0.28), its sea open over 0.44 of its dayside (almost half). KNOWN: its night's cloud lies by latitude, none at its equator and all of it from 40 degrees, the eddies' share falling toward its equator as within a spinning world's cell where its overturning runs from its night to its day. Titan: its methane's evaporation takes 0.56 W/m² where 0.48 (its ground's sunlight 0.35), 35 kg/m² a year where 30 (the models' 5 to 45): its cell reaches past its poles, the eddies' share under a half everywhere, and its drift's layer holds 0.06 of its column, so that its methane rains nearer where it rose; with the old transport and the new shedding it takes 0.47. Held under 0.6 W/m² where 0.5. Found with it, and older than it: its ground has no sea for its runoff, 18 of the 35 kg/m², and over the body its evaporation stands over its rain less its runoff by its runoff, year on year, which only its buckets' floor at none can give back: a source its year's balance has stood on since F268, for its methane's own cycle to remove (the bucket's step, and a sea-less body's lakes).
- **Tried, not kept.** Bretherton et al. (2004) give two laws of rain by a column's saturation: a day's, exp(15.6 (r - 0.603)), which the moisture uses, and a month's, exp(11.4 (r - 0.522)). The model's column is a climatological one, and the month's law at ERA5's 0.67 over the equator's seas gives 5.4 mm a day where GPCP's 5.3. In the model it brought the columns' saturation from 0.63 to 0.73 down to 0.63 to 0.69 (ERA5's 0.50 to 0.67), moved the clouds little, and cooled the year 0.9 K through its drier clear sky: left for the clear sky's own column.
- **What remains.** The tropics rain a quarter to a third over Earth's because their sea gives too much: 5.17 mm a day within ten degrees of the equator where ERA5's 4.17, and too little from 20 to 30 degrees, 3.79 where 4.72. Earth's seas carry some 30 to 40 W/m² away from the equator in the overturning their trades drive, equal and opposite to the air's drift (Held 2001), and the ITCZ's clouds shade it by 50 more; the model's sea carries none of the first and its stated law lays none of the second. The subtropics, wetter than Earth's by 0.4 to 0.6 mm a day, lay clouds that keep up to 8.8 W/m² more than CERES's. From 50 to 70 degrees the rain stands four tenths under Earth's.
- **The invariants.** Fifteen planted bugs, each caught by a check: the drift carrying its whole column; its layer's share of the vapour without its exponential; the eddies' air without its latent heat; all of the transport the eddies' within the cell; the cell's edges not moved with the thermal equator; the mixing on the vapour's column and not its surface air's; the sea giving into its column's saturation, in the moisture, in the cycle's heat and in the moisture's contact (the last two by the stated year's checks: its sea 0.25 K over its air, and 1,604 kg/m² of rain a year); the drift not brought to the wind's strength, up the pressure's gradient, or without its part along the latitudes; the bucket shedding only what overflows it, or by its fill and not its square; and the wind's vapour taken from the place downwind. The last and the bucket's overflow passed the tests first written, so one was added for them (`a_wind_carries_its_vapour_downwind_to_a_land_that_sheds`): a strip of latitudes with one band of open sea under a steady wind, whose land downwind gains the layer's share of the sea's own column at the edge's wind, by hand within 0.01 (the land's own column would give 0.14 less), and sheds the square of its buckets' fill of its rain though they never fill.
- **The finish.** At 2048 the finish's Earth rains 1,005 kg/m² a year where 1,214 (GPCP 980, ERA5 1,070), 800 on its land where 854, its land shedding 263 where 352; 0.179 of its land stands arid and 0.070 hyper-arid where 0.315 and 0.218 (UNEP 1992: 0.196 and 0.075), 0.176 of it drains to no sea where 0.371 (Earth's some 0.18), and its largest river carries 82,500 m³/s where 160,700. Its land from 20 to 40 degrees takes 520 to 740 mm a year where 70 to 160, its land within 10 degrees of its equator 1,480 where 2,170. KNOWN, a regression: its widest tropical continent, 7,000 km along its equator, stands arid over its western half, 0.20 to 0.23 of the land within 10 degrees of its equator arid where under 0.02, its trades' rain spent over its eastern half and no eddies' share there to bring its western sea's; and 0.07 to 0.12 of its land from 30 to 40 degrees is arid, its subtropics' seas a quarter wetter than Earth's. TRAPPIST-1e's land stands dry under its star, a regression: 252 kg/m² a year on its land where 993, its largest river 2,000 m³/s where 935,000, its rain its open sea's own evaporation where it gathered under its star. One cause with Titan's: the drift carries its vapour in the layer its ground's friction holds, the measure of an inflow the spin limits, Earth's trades' (its vapour across 10 degrees within the measured 0.1 to 1.0e9 kg/s); an overturning the spin does not limit, a land's or a day's low drawing air from its sea or its night, flows in deeper.
- **Next.** The order is the user's (asked 2026-11-16): the overturnings the spin does not limit, the vapour a land's, a day's or a slow world's cell draws in at depth (the finish's tropical continents' western halves, TRAPPIST-1e's land, Titan's transport), researched first (Neelin and Held 1987's moisture convergence by the column's energy export; Lindzen and Nigam 1987); the sea's overturning under its trades (Held 2001), for its equator's evaporation; and M75, the coupling on, ALBEDO_BENEATH, WATER_CLOUD_DEPTH and CLOUD_SUNLIGHT kept only as the zero-dimensional balance's and the first year's estimate, the finish's clouds in the sky with the cell's air.
- **The suite.** 431 tests with the whole-body tests in 117 minutes, 429 passing as first run: Titan's evaporation stood over its hold and the locked world's night over M66's clear one. With those two checks re-anchored as above, the Earth-like's bounds tightened and the strip's test added, the three years' tests were run again and pass (131 minutes, beside the planted bugs' builds); the fast tier, 392 tests, in 3.5 minutes; the tier now 432. ## The seventy-fifth milestone: the overturning's vapour

- **F379.** The overturning's vapour by the heat it carries over its gross stability.
- **F380.** The land's potential evaporation Penman's.
- **F381.** Two years over lands held: tropical continents, and a land under a locked world's star.
- **F382.** The water's state; KNOWN.

How it went:

- **The user's choice.** M74 left three regressions: the finish's widest tropical land arid across its equator, TRAPPIST-1e's land dry under its star with its rivers gone, and Titan's evaporation up. Asked for the order, the user chose the inflow first (2026-11-16), before the sea's overturning and the coupling.
- **The research.** "Moisture inflow of direct circulations" (research notes): Siler, Roe and Armour's (2018) moist energy balance model gives the Hadley cell's vapour from its heat, psi = F / (1.06 h(0) - h(x)), its latent flux -psi L q; Held (2001) the same with the mass from the Ekman transport, which is M74's layer. No energy balance model uses it in two dimensions, over a land or on a locked world. The anchors: a land under a locked world's star stays wet at its middle and desert on its flanks (Lewis et al. 2018; Macdonald et al. 2022); 14 models keep a 45 degree equatorial continent wet when its soil cannot dry (Biasutti et al. 2021); the Amazon's recycled share rises from a sixth in its east to a half in its west (Eltahir and Bras 1994).
- **A world with lands, in minutes.** The Earth-like's year cannot tell where on a land its rain falls, and a render takes 25 minutes. Two diagnostic years, 14 and 9 minutes, gave the lands' rain by longitude and by the distance from the point under the star, and now stand as whole-body tests (F381).
- **What they showed first.** On the tropical continents M74's rain fell from 3,800 mm a year at the east coast to 400 at the west along the equator, and from 20 to 30 degrees from 1,500 to 30: the subtropics as Earth's, the equator too dry in the west. But the potential evaporation there stood at 2,700 to 6,700 mm a year, the most where it was driest: the classes read arid where 400 to 1,000 mm fell. On the locked world a round land under the star was wet, 3,300 kg/m² a year, nearly all of it its own evaporation; the render's land took 2,000 near its star and its potential 5,000.
- **The closure.** As a trial behind a switch, then built: the heat through each link, by the share the eddies leave, over the stability, carries the cold place's surface air's vapour to the warm. The Earth-like's ten stated checks pass; its vapour across 10 degrees 0.57e9 kg/s where 0.37e9 (Peixoto and Oort 0.5 to 0.6e9). The locked land's middle rains 14,150 kg/m² a year, its land giving back a third, its coast 3,960 and the sea beyond 1,120: rain nearest the star, brought in, shed.
- **Tried, not kept.** The drift's layer kept beside the overturning, by the eddies' share of the transport (their winds' own drift): the Earth-like's belt rains 5.83 mm a day where 5.60 and its vapour crosses 10 degrees at 0.73e9 kg/s, its subtropics the nearer Earth's (1.99 mm a day; GPCP 1.92) and its 30 to 40 degrees the farther (2.24; 2.56). One count of an overturning's inflow was kept, its heat's.
- **Penman's potential.** The land's potential evaporation is what a wet ground would give with the heat the ground has for its air; over the tropical continents 1,400 to 2,350 mm a year, by its seas' 1,300 to 1,900. With it the narrow continent's equator reads humid from its second place on, the wide one's humid over its eastern half and no drier than semi-arid but at its west coast's place.
- **The renders, before the tier.** As M74 taught. Earth: 1,001 kg/m² of rain a year (1,005 in M74), 788 on its land where 800, its land shedding 271 where 263; its equator's lands arid over 0.04 to 0.07 of them where 0.20 to 0.23, its deserts on its lands' subtropical wests and interiors; 0.096 of its land arid and 0.039 hyper-arid by Penman's potential where 0.179 and 0.070 by M74's; 0.134 of it draining to no sea where 0.176, its lakes 0.037 of it where 0.034; its largest river 124,500 m³/s where 82,500. TRAPPIST-1e: its land's rain 390 kg/m² a year where 252, 7,000 within 10 degrees of its star and 26 from 30 to 45 degrees away; its rain no longer the shape of its open sea but a round maximum under its star, over land and sea alike, the same 365 kg/m² a year over the body where 375: the same rain, gathered nearer its star; its largest river 264,000 m³/s where 2,000; 0.51 of its land arid, most of it far from its star (Macdonald et al. 2022: most of a large land hot and dry).
- **The other bodies.** Titan: its methane's evaporation takes 0.52 W/m² where 0.56 (M74) and 0.48 before it, 33 kg/m² a year; by Penman's potential, its ground's heat for its air a fraction of a watt, its rain stands over a fifth of its potential evaporation everywhere, so that none of its land reads arid where 0.31 did: its tropics' rain is what stands too high (KNOWN, with its sea-less runoff's return by its buckets' floor: its methane's own cycle). The locked ocean world's first year: 12,200 kg/m² a year under its star where 5,900 (M74) and 9,400 (M70), its night's low cloud over 0.37 of it where 0.60, keeping 2.6 W/m² where 4.1 (THAI's models' -5.0 to 0.3).
- **Under their own clouds.** The Earth-like under its own clouds stands at 284.5 K where 284.7 in M74, reflecting 0.330 as before (Earth's 0.29), its last years' means 284.82, 284.77, 284.68 and 284.82 K; its polar ice still grows after 80 years, 40 m of it, its polar night's air 28.5 K over its ice's top. TRAPPIST-1e under its own clouds stands at 249.9 K where 249.6 (THAI 232 to 246), its dayside at 267.3 where 267.1 (261 to 268), reflecting 0.238 where 0.258 (0.22 to 0.28), its sea open over 0.44 of its dayside. The bounds M73 loosened stand as M74 left them.
- **The invariants.** Sixteen planted bugs, each caught by a fast check: the overturning's vapour carried from its warm place to its cold; the warm place's vapour carried; the stability the rising end's everywhere; all of the transport the overturning's; its heat without the share the eddies leave; each link counted from both its places; the rising air the body's most energetic and not its rain's (twice: its rain's weights at none, and the step before's rain never kept); CELL_STABILITY a tenth; the energy without its vapour's; the drift across the isobars carrying its column again; Penman's without its sharing; the land's potential without its ground's warmth, or by what it would give all wet; and the overturning's shorter steps, each carrying the whole step's mass or none taken. The last two passed the first tests: the year's test now runs a transport a hundred times as strong, which moves many times a column a step, and holds that its vapour is kept (planted, 2,830 and 12,215 kg/m² of rain a year against 1,051 given).
- **KNOWN.** The Earth-like's tropics rain a third over Earth's (5.60 mm a day from 15 south to 15 north; GPCP 4.07, ERA5 4.66) and its storm tracks four tenths under, as in M74: its sea gives a quarter too much at its equator, and its transport at 40 degrees carries too little. The wide continent's equator stands arid at its west coast's place (0.13) and semi-arid to sub-humid over its western half; models differ there by whether the soil can dry. The finish's Earth's arid lands are a half of Earth's share (UNEP: 0.172 of all land arid or hyper-arid): its lands are few from 20 to 40 degrees north, and the Earth-like's subtropics rain a tenth to a sixth more than Earth's (2.26 mm a day from 20 to 30 degrees; GPCP 1.92, ERA5 2.09). The cell's share of the transport is Earth's law by its reach; on TRAPPIST-1e one model finds a weaker convergence under its star than on Proxima b (Sergeev et al. 2020), so its rain's maximum may be drawn too tight. CELL_STABILITY is a fit to Earth's air; no other air's is published. On cold land where rain and potential evaporation both stand under some 100 mm a year, the aridity's class follows the coarse places' cells (TRAPPIST-1e's far southern land, humid and hyper-arid in blocks).
- **Next.** The sea's overturning under its trades (Held 2001): the equator's sea gives a quarter more than Earth's and its tropics rain a third more. Then the coupling on.
- **The suite.** 435 tests with the whole-body tests in 115 minutes, all passing, the two lands' years among them (30 and 26 minutes); the fast tier, 393 tests, in 3.1 minutes. FINISH_VERSION 237.

## The seventy-sixth milestone: the wind through the weather

- **F383.** The ground's drag reads the speed through the weather: friction, work, stress, belts.
- **F384.** The friction layer re-anchored on Earth's measured friction rates.
- **F385.** The trades' state; KNOWN.

How it went:

- **The sea's overturning first.** In the user's order the sea's overturning came next (research notes, "Sea overturning under the trades": Codron 2012's Ekman layer for slab oceans, Held 2001's return water). A diagnosis of the Earth-like's stress first: its trades laid -0.031 N/m² on its sea at 12 to 16 degrees where QuikSCAT's -0.048 north and -0.057 south, its flow at 4 to 8 degrees mostly toward the equator, and no easterlies on its equator: built on that wind the sea's Ekman flow would converge and sink on the equator, where Earth's rises. The user chose the trades first, then their cheap fixes, the momentum the friction layer takes from the air above it recorded for later (2026-11-16).
- **The research.** "Trade winds and the boundary layer" (research notes): zonal means of the wind, its stress, its direction and its friction rates by latitude, computed from QuikSCAT 2000 to 2008, ICOADS and NCEP's 1991 to 2020 means; Earth's surface dissipation some 1.0 W/m², its kinetic energy generated at 1.9 to 2.5.
- **What it showed.** The trades' stress is the mean wind's times the speed through the weather, 7.5 m/s in the trades about a mean of 4.4: the model laid it at the mean's speed. Its friction used the body's mean wind at every place over some 250 m: with each place's own speed the measured rates ask for some 420 m. With one friction for every place its torque balanced its winds, not its stress: its westerlies' stress stood two thirds over its trades'.
- **Prototypes.** Four winds side by side, then three depths, each through the stated year's rain checks: the gusts in the stress alone gave -0.040 N/m² at 12 to 16 degrees; spent from the same work, which the gusts share, -0.035; the depth from 0.04 to 0.06 of the scale height turned the trades from 0.83 to 0.53 across at 16 degrees. A heat engine half again as strong, a trial, -0.047: the work's estimate is the next lever after the momentum from aloft.
- **The belts.** With the gusts sharing the work the westerlies blew 7 m/s where 9.5 and their belts, spaced by their own mean wind, ended before 55 degrees, where Earth's reach 60 (F285's check); spaced by their flow's whole rms speed, as Rhines read it, they reach past 55 again.
- **By the measurements.** The Earth-like's stress on its sea at 10, 15 and 20 degrees -0.032, -0.037 and -0.030 N/m², 0.79, 0.71 and 0.58 of QuikSCAT's two hemispheres' mean, where -0.027, -0.032 and -0.022; its northward stress over its eastward 1.13, 0.73 and 0.48 where Earth's 0.54, 0.52 and 0.53, and 1.27, 0.83 and 0.62 before; its torque balanced in its stress on each side to a few parts in ten thousand of its gross; its westerlies' strongest stress 0.100 N/m² at 42 degrees, some 7 m/s where 9.5; its sea's winds through the weather 5.38 m/s from 60 south to 60 north (ICOADS 7.1 to 7.6). ## The seventy-seventh milestone: the sea's overturning

- **F386.** The sea's Ekman flow from the wind's stress (Codron 2012).
- **F387.** The overturning and its return water (Held 2001), in two dimensions; only where its surface water flows from warmer to colder.

How it went:

- **The design.** The research notes ("Sea overturning under the trades") gave Codron's (2012) Ekman layer for slab oceans, the one published scheme, and Held's (2001) and Klinger and Marotzke's (2000) return water. A first thought, the sea's flow the mirror of the air's drift, holds only where the spin rules: near the equator and on a slow spinner the sea runs down the wind, the air's way and not against it. The sea's own friction is Codron's, anchored on the drifters' downwind band at Earth's equator. The return water's temperature needs a basin's integral in Klinger and Marotzke's form; a steady layer beneath, fed by the water each place gives down and carried back along the return flow, gives the same law on any layout.
- **Pure functions.** The Ekman flow, the overturning, the return water and the heat step stand in the currents' module, each checked by hand: a whole-sea Earth under 0.05 N/m² carries 66 Sv across 10 degrees, the return water along a strip stands at the subduction's mean by its weights, the heat is kept through a step.
- **The first build, and what the user's look at the renders found.** Built as Klinger and Marotzke's law alone, the cells ran whichever way the wind drove the surface water. The Earth-like's equator's sea gave 4.78 mm a day where 5.02 and its tropics rained 5.16 where 5.41; TRAPPIST-1e's sea under its star stood 3.3 K cooler. But its snapshot, which the user asked to see, held blocks of open sea about its night's pole and an eye of open sea no longer round. A diagnosis by the distance from its star: its wind drives its surface water toward its star, 30 to 57 Sv going down in each band within 60 degrees of it, its sea 276 to 291 K there, and some 400 Sv rising from 75 degrees on at 273 to 278 K under seas frozen at 271.4 K, 10 to 36 W/m² of heat brought up beneath its ice. Water warmer than the sea over it cannot stand beneath it in a sea of one salt; on Earth's seas the subtropical cells' return water is the colder, Klinger and Marotzke's law safe there.
- **Tried, not kept.** The return water held no warmer than the sea it rises under, the heat it would have brought given back where water went down by each place's share: the polar cells' heat went down at the equator's convergence, the tropics' rain 5.54 mm a day. Neither is the physics: warm water driven toward a warmer sea gathers there and stands, the sea's surface sloping against the wind, as the trades heap the western Pacific's warm pool, and drives no overturning.
- **Kept.** The overturning runs only where its surface water flows from a warmer sea to a colder: its water goes down where it is the colder, and rises colder than the sea it rises under, the heat kept without a correction. Earth's subtropical cells pass; the trades' frictional convergence on the equator, a locked world's flow toward its star and the subpolar cells, which Earth's salt holds and this sea's warmth alone cannot, turn over none. The Earth-like's cells carry 0.13 PW away from the equator each side (Klinger and Marotzke 0.39 north, 0.64 south), the band within 11 degrees losing 2.7 W/m² where theirs 11.6, its upwelling cooling its sea 4.4 W/m² there; its equator's sea gives 4.94 mm a day where 5.02 and its tropics rain 5.29 where 5.41: most of the first build's gain was the cells that cannot stand.

## The eightieth milestone: the wind's momentum from aloft, tried

No F-number: tried, not kept.

How it went:

- **The plan.** M76 left the Earth-like's trades two thirds of Earth's stress, turned twice too far across at 10 degrees (northward over eastward 1.12 where 0.54) and calm on the equator (Earth's -2.1 m/s), the sea's overturning (M77) waiting on them. The research notes ("Trade winds and the boundary layer") gave the cause as the air above the friction layer, which feeds it its momentum: Stevens et al.'s (2002) mixed layer, f k x U = -grad p / rho + e_e (U_T - U) - e_d U, its rates fitted to COADS and NCEP, 2e-5 /s entraining and 1.5e-5 dragging, U_T the wind at 850 hPa (Back and Bretherton 2009: QuikSCAT's winds within r = 0.98).
- **The air above.** Stevens et al. read U_T from reanalyses; the model has no free troposphere. Back and Bretherton find the surface zonal winds the free troposphere's pressure gradients mixed down and the convergence the boundary layer's own temperatures; the model's pressure splits the same way, so the air above felt the latitudes' belts and not the departures along each latitude. A probe on the aquaplanet of the wind's tests, its warmth shifted 10 degrees north and south: the air above barely braked piled the seasons' geostrophic easterlies up at 2 to 4 degrees, the trades' stress strongest at 4; braked toward rest at the entrainment's rate it kept them at 15 degrees and crossed the isobars by 24 degrees at 15, inside the trades' measured 21 to 27.
- **The Earth-like's year, braked toward rest.** Every check held, once the wind's drift (M74) counted all of the latitudes' flow across their isobars (the first run left out the air above's own drift and carried the column's vapour to the equator twice, 8.8 mm a day of rain within 10 degrees). Its trades at 10, 15 and 20 degrees crossed by 0.92, 0.56 and 0.34 where 1.12, 0.73 and 0.47 (Earth 0.54, 0.52 and 0.53); their stress -0.036, -0.038 and -0.027 N/m² where -0.032, -0.037 and -0.029 (QuikSCAT -0.041, -0.053, -0.051); the sea's Ekman flow across 10 degrees 21e9 kg/s where 17 (Earth's by its sea's share 43); its rain, its ice and its warmth as before; its equator -0.00 m/s.
- **Why not kept.** Braked toward rest, the air above lost momentum to nothing, and the layer, given the air above's drift toward the equator and turning it with the spin, outran it by a tenth, where Earth's trades blow faster at 850 hPa than at the ground. Exchanging momentum with the layer alone, the ground's drag the column's one sink, the pair put the trades' strongest stress at 6 degrees where Earth's stands at 15 to 17, and crossed the isobars a third to a half of Earth's at 15 to 25 degrees. Neither form is an improvement of the model as a whole (the user's test, 2026-11-17).
- **What it found.** The equator stays calm in every linear form: on a world the same at every longitude nothing drives a zonal wind where the spin's f is none, the air above's no more than the layer's, and Earth's equatorial easterlies are the Walker cell's, 83 percent of their stress from the Pacific (the research notes), a zonal asymmetry of the sea's warmth. The trades' strength is set by the heat engine's work, the model's 1.3 W/m² where Earth's measured 1.9 to 2.5: the stress some 30 percent stronger at Earth's. The trades' turn's change with latitude, Earth's constant some 27 degrees from 10 to 25, wants the air above's own dynamics, its wind and its stability through the cell.
- **KNOWN, as M76 left them.** The trades' stress and turn, the equator's calm and the westerlies' latitude; the patch in research/patches (m80-wind-momentum-from-aloft.patch).
- **Next.** The coupling on, the user's order.

## The eighty-fifth milestone: the free air a locked world's day reads

- **F398.** The free air held to its cell's rising air's within the equatorial waves' reach.
- **F399.** The free air no colder than its cell's angular momentum lets it fall.

How it went:

- **The state found.** Since M82 the inversion's free air stood at its latitude's own air (F394). On a world locked to its star its latitude's air is its night's with its day's: TRAPPIST-1e's day under cloud 0.59 where 0.74, its ring of low cloud beyond its eye mostly gone, and under its own clouds it reflected 0.201 where 0.240 (THAI's 0.22 to 0.28).
- **The research** (the research notes, "Free air and the equatorial waves' reach"). The equatorial Kelvin wave's amplitude falls from the equator as exp(-y² / (2 L²)), L = sqrt(c / beta) (Matsuno 1966, eq. 15; Gill 1980, eq. 4.3); Earth's first baroclinic mode runs at 44 to 53 m/s and reaches 12.7 degrees (Kiladis et al. 2009), c = N D / pi under a lid (Gill 1980, eq. 2.1); the waves' adjustment of the temperature is "confined to within an equatorial deformation radius of the equator" (Showman et al. 2013). On locked worlds the free air stands within a few kelvin from day to night over a surface tens of kelvin apart, the night's inversion reaching 600 to 700 hPa (Joshi et al. 1997; Merlis and Schneider 2010; Yang and Abbot 2014; Sergeev et al. 2020). THAI's four models' days under cloud 0.41 to 0.81 (Sergeev et al. 2022, figure 6d, read from the figure). The locked worlds' papers put c at N H, some 140 m/s, three times the first mode's: no source derives the first mode's for a locked world.
- **The waves' hold (F398).** The wave's speed from the sky's own stratification: Earth's 48.0 m/s, reaching 13.0 degrees. The Earth-like's air at 700 hPa within 0.7 K of ERA-Interim's in every band to 60 degrees where 2.1, its equator's band 283.1 K where 285.1 (ERA's 283.0): its equator's own air stands too warm (KNOWN, M72), and its cell's rising air's, the rain's own places', is the free air's there. TRAPPIST-1e's day under cloud 0.63 where 0.59: its waves reach 24 degrees.
- **Its middle latitudes.** Beyond the waves' reach its free air was still its latitude's own, tens of kelvin under its rising air's, where its models' tropospheres stand within 3.5 to 9.6 K from day to night at every latitude. The Kelvin wave is the narrowest of the waves that spread the rising air's warmth; the wave of the locked worlds' papers, three times as fast, would stand the Earth-like's free air some 2 K over ERA-Interim's from 30 to 40 degrees (computed).
- **The cell's fall (F399).** What holds a slow spinner's free air level is its spin's square: air that keeps its angular momentum from the equator blows at Omega a sin² / cos, and the temperature balancing that wind falls by theta_0 u² / (2 g H) (Held and Hou 1980). No air from the equator blows faster aloft, so none stands colder than that under the rising air's. Earth's fall 3.5 K by 20 degrees and 18.6 by 30, past what its air falls by: the Earth-like's bands as under F398 alone, to the tenth of a kelvin. TRAPPIST-1e's free air 283.3 K at its equator, its rising air's, 279.4 at 45 degrees and 257.2 at 80, where its latitudes' own stand at 261.2, 259.2 and 257.1 K. Its day under cloud 0.69, its night 0.33, 0.26 of it its ice cloud.

## The ninetieth milestone: the plan's shape

- No finding: the plan, the README and a check of them (the user, 2026-11-19).

How it went:

It is now a table of every phase and sub-phase with its state, Done, Under way or Planned, and a "Now" line, and changes only when a gate passes. `crates/exogen-core/tests/roadmap.rs` holds it to PLAN.md's roadmap: each heading's State line, a phase's state its sub-phases', the Now line the sub-phases under way. Four slips planted, each caught: a gate marked in the plan alone, a State line missing, a phase against its sub-phases, a finished sub-phase in the Now line.
- **The roadmap, one style for its purpose.** Every phase and sub-phase is written the same way: its State, what it Builds (each item done ending in the findings that did it), what is Open, what was Tried and not kept, its Gate, its Presets and its Report. It holds the plan and what is left of it; the milestones' narratives are the reports' (80,000 characters to 50,000), every finding of 5B and 5C placed under an item. 5B is marked done (its gate passed 2026-11-02, the plan still had it under way).

- **Phase 6N, the noise pass.** Principle 16's one pass over every body after Phase 6 (the user, 2026-10-30) given its place: noise modulated by the processes' fields, the regional shades, outlines warped, one calibration on every body together. Its gate: one warp for every map, no point moved past its contact's own transition width, the units' areas and the materials' shares held (the user asked how a warp could leave the maps unchanged; it cannot, and the gate now says what it may change).
- **The first crust and the first shell (the user).** One law for every material (section 4.15): what a body's birth melted against its materials' melting points, what froze first and whether it floated, whether its rock settled before its ocean froze, and a freezing ocean cracking its shell. Today's Callisto is derived fully sorted (a 921 km layer of water over a 19 percent iron core) under a crust as it accreted, and Ganymede's lanes are light by the rule that renewed ice is clean (F126): the law gives both a cause. Its icy half opens 5E in three steps, the melt at birth checked against the moments of inertia, dust from outside (Ganymede's dark terrain needs it once its first shell is clean), and the icy first shell; its rocky half stays in Phase 6.
- **Earth's sea ice, diagnosed (M88's open item).** On the test world under its own clouds, the Earth-like's 50 to 70 degrees stand 5.5 to 6.5 K under the stated law's and its seas at 50 to 60 degrees ice over 0.41 of the year where 0.05. Its poleward heat transport is Earth's (6.0 PW at 30 degrees, 3.4 at 60, CERES's 5.8 and 3.4); the stated law's was a quarter short, its albedo fit darker than Earth's oceans at 50 to 60 degrees. Under its own clouds the open sea at 40 to 50 degrees reflects 0.40 where CERES measures 0.35, and the ice's brightness adds to the cold. ISCCP-H's own clouds through the model's optics over-reflect there too (1.30 at 40 to 50 north against 1.07 in the tropics); a cloud's patchiness by the sun's height moves its light by 1 to 2 percent; the model's clouds hold half the water AMSR2 measures (45 g/m² at 40 to 50 degrees where 90) with drops near the measured 11 to 14 um and a liquid share by temperature as measured. The fix is the next milestone's (research notes: "Cloud reflectance under a low sun", "Cloud water and drops by latitude").

## The ninety-second milestone: the air's profile as tables by height

- **F404.** The air's profile as tables by height (AIR_PROFILE_SHARE, AIR_PROFILE_WAVELENGTHS).

Each level gives its altitude, pressure, temperature, density, molar mass, water vapour's share, Rayleigh scattering at 440, 550 and 680 nm, and a methane haze's extinction. A first design hung one column over each group's mean ground; its Earth's mean stood within 1.6 K of the area mean's from 5 to 11 km. The area mean was kept, as it holds what each place's year read.
- **Against measured profiles** (research notes, "Reference atmosphere profiles"; the model's mean table by height, the references single soundings but Earth's):

| body | reference | heights | model minus measured |
|---|---|---|---|
| Earth | US Standard Atmosphere 1976, a mid-latitude profile | 0 to 2 km | -0.5 to +1.9 K |
| | | 5 to 11 km | +7.8 to +12.4 K |
| | | 15 to 25 km | -2.5 to -4.7 K |
| | | 30 to 60 km | +14 to +63 K |
| | | 60 to 80 km | +37 to -21 K, levels alternating above 55 km |
| Venus | Pioneer Venus Large probe | 0 to 35 km | -2.7 to +7.6 K |
| | | 40 to 64 km | -13 to -72 K |
| | Pioneer Venus Day probe | 70 to 80 km | -36 to -8 K, the skin's 192.6 K |
| Titan | Huygens HASI | 0 to 25 km | +5.8 to +8.2 K |
| | | 44 to 70 km | -3 to -44 K: its coldest 58 K near 60 km, Huygens's 70.4 at 44 |
| | | 80 to 130 km | +10 to +28 K |
| | | 146 to 200 km | -6 to +3 K |
| Mars | Pathfinder's entry, at night | 9 to 10 km | -9 to -10 K |
| | | 15 to 30 km | -22 to -31 K |
| | | 60 to 120 km | +49 to +84 K, the skin's 181 K |

- **The light.** Earth's Rayleigh depth up its table 0.0967 at 550 nm where Bodhaine et al. 1999 give 0.0971; 0.2362 at 440 where 0.2426, and 0.0414 at 680 where 0.0410: the inverse fourth power without the refractive index's dispersion. Titan's haze 7.08 deep at 550 nm up its table, its law's 7.03.
- **What they say.** Earth's air from 5 to 11 km is 8 to 12 K warm, the cause not yet traced. Its warmest tenth's air at 500 hPa stands at 276 K over its 304.5 K ground, and every place's air aloft reads the cell's (M73). Earth's upper stratosphere is warm, as known (F348: carbon dioxide's line cores). Above 55 km its levels alternate warm and cold, likely because each thin layer emits at its two levels' mean, which such a mode leaves unchanged. Venus's 40 to 64 km lack the deck's own heating. Mars's middle air lacks its dust's heating, and its upper air stands at the skin's temperature where carbon dioxide's cooling holds the real one 100 to 130 K. Titan's ground has been known warm since F303. All are in 5C's Open list.
- **The checks.** Fast: `an_air_s_heights_follow_its_hydrostatic_balance` (the heights against the inverse-square law's closed form to 1e-6, and a flat gravity's scale height); `an_air_s_table_stands_on_its_own_profile` (Earth's table: its ground at the surface pressure, the gas law at every level, its vapour, its Rayleigh depth within 3 percent of the column's; Titan's haze within 5 percent of its law). Whole-body: `an_air_s_tables_are_its_places_mean` on Titan (each tenth's area, the warm places over the cold, each table its places' area mean) and `an_air_s_tables_stand_over_its_grounds` on the Earth-like (cold under mean under warm on the ground and at half the pressure, the cold tenth's air aloft more than 5 K over its own column's, the airless bodies with none).
- **The invariants.** Six bugs planted, each caught:
  - the heights under a flat gravity, by the heights' check, and by Titan's haze, 6.52 deep up its table where 7.03;
  - the density by the dry air's molar mass, by the gas law;
  - the haze without gravity's fall with height, at 7.81;
  - the warm and cold tenths swapped, by Titan's warm over cold;
  - the tenths' airs averaged by place and not by area, by Titan's mean, 0.005 K off;
  - the tables without the cell's air aloft, by the Earth-like's cold tenth, 213.6 K at half its pressure where its own column's 213.7.
- **KNOWN.** As above, in 5C's Open list; not yet in the tables: the gases' visible absorption, the hazes' single-scattering albedo and asymmetry, the haze by place (5G).
