## The ninety-third milestone: Earth's air aloft

- **F405.** Each level balanced over its own halves of the layers about it.
- **F406.** The convection entrains (CONVECTION_ENTRAINMENT); WATER_CLOUD_DEPTH set again.

How it went:

- **The state found.** M92's tables put Earth's mean air 8 to 12 K warm from 5 to 11 km against the US Standard Atmosphere, and its levels above 55 km alternating; the user chose the column's remaining parts in turn, this first (2026-11-20).
- **The reference.** The US Standard is a mid-latitude profile, some 6 K colder at 5 km than the globe's mean. ERA-Interim's annual means (the M68 download, by ten degrees, the globe and its warmest and coldest tenths; the research notes, "Reference atmosphere profiles") put the warmth at 400 to 250 hPa, 5 to 6 K, and in the warmest tenth, 9 to 15 K.
- **Where it came from.** The column's air depends only on its ground and its cell's air, so it was read as a curve of its ground against ERA's bands (`print_the_air_by_latitude`). Over a 300 K ground its air at 300 to 150 hPa stood 5 K over ERA's 0 to 10 degrees: its convection lifted its surface air undilute, through a convecting layer aloft mixed on the same lapse. The mid-latitudes took the cell's warmth aloft through M73's law. The tropics' ground itself 3 to 4 K warm adds to the warmest tenth (the equator's peak, in 5C's Open list).
- **The entrainment (F406).** Romps's (2016) zero-buoyancy plume, a = 0.2, his own for Earth's tropics (the research notes, "Entraining plume lapse rate"): the moist adiabat's lapse with the saturated vapour over 1 + a, the dry adiabat where nothing condenses. Over a 300 K sea (ERA's 0 to 10 degrees' air at 299.2 K, 0.85 K under its sea by the tropical moorings) its air within 0.5 K of ERA's from 850 to 300 hPa and 1.8 K under at 200, where 5.0 and 4.3 warm before. The air aloft at the cell's stability over 40 to 50 and 60 to 70 degrees' annual grounds within 1.4 K of ERA's at 500 and 300 hPa, where 3.8 to 6.5 warm. AFGL's subarctic winter, M73's anchor, now 7 K over its 500 hPa air: its inversion, which a year's mean ground does not hold; the check reads the annual zonal means.
- **The alternating levels (F405).** In a thin layer the linear source emits at its two levels' mean, so levels warm and cold by turns changed no level's heating over the two whole layers about it: Earth's 288 K column turned 18 times above 80 hPa, by up to 6 K. Each level is now balanced between the middles of the layers about it, the flux at a middle exact for the linear source, the levels' fluxes as before. None alternate; the troposphere over a 300 K ground stands as before to 0.2 K up to 150 hPa. With it Venus's own radiative-convective solve converges in 21 steps where M65 found a slow 2-delta mode: the thick air's own profile next.
- **The anchors.** The colder air aloft lets out less, and the stated clouds' law left the zero-dimensional Earth at 292.2 K: set again, 0.13487 u^0.1673, its b held. Fitting the slope's 2.1 W/m² a kelvin again took b to 0.037, a law that no longer thins its clouds with its air's vapour: Mars's 0.01 kg/m² of vapour held 0.18 of grey depth where 0.074 and stood 2.3 K warmer. Held, the slope 1.92, within the 1.6 to 2.2 measured (Warren and Schneider 1979); the clear sky at 291.6 K 264.5 W/m² (CERES 267), the clouds keeping 25.0 (28). Venus's and Titan's anchors stand.
- **The renders** (1024, M92's maps beside them):

| body | | M92 | M93 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Earth (draft) | mean, equator, poles | 286.1, 301.2, 243.4 K | 288.0, 302.8, 246.6 K |
| | sea ice, albedo | 0.095, 0.305 | 0.079, 0.298 |
| | rain, kg/m² a year | 1,065 | 1,106 |
| | its years under its own clouds | 291.4, 288.8, 287.9 K | 294.8, 290.6, 289.7 K |
| | mean air minus ERA at 400 to 250 hPa | +4.7 to +6.4 K | +3.2 to +4.8 K |
| TRAPPIST-1e (draft) | mean, sea ice, albedo | 249.3 K, 0.5629, 0.239 | 249.3 K, 0.5634, 0.236 |
| Mars | mean; residual CO2 cap | 204.9 K; 0.030 % | 204.5 K; 0.015 % |
| Titan | mean; evaporation | 97.44 K; 11.9 kg/m² a year | 97.36 K; 10.8 |
| Venus | mean | 735.49 K | 735.57 K |

- **What they say.** Earth's surface warms under its own clouds by its air's lapse, its draft's mean now Earth's 288 K, its sea ice nearer the measured 0.069, its rain the more. TRAPPIST-1e's eye keeps its shape. Mars's residual CO2 cap, marginal since 5B's gate, halves with its year 0.4 K the colder. Titan's rivers turn on its 0.09 K: its closed land 0.91 of it where 0.68, its largest river 328 m³/s where 996 (Titan's methane cycle, in 5C's Open list). The derived means: Earth 291.6 K, Venus 737.0, Mars 217.3 (217.6), Titan 97.30 (97.33).
- **KNOWN.** The warmest tenth 9 to 13 K warm aloft over a ground 3.6 K warm (the equator's peak); the stratosphere's floor at the cell's tropopause everywhere (ERA's 192 K over the tropics, 220 over the poles: the stratosphere's own overturning); the upper stratosphere's warmth (F348).
- **The checks.** Fast: `a_thin_air_s_levels_do_not_alternate` (Earth's column from 100 hPa up, at most one turn of its curvature); `the_net_flux_s_slopes_meet_its_differences` reads the middles' slopes too; `the_column_lifts_its_surface_air` reads the column's own air over a 300 K sea against ERA's 0 to 10 degrees within 2.5 K to 200 hPa; `an_air_aloft_keeps_its_cell_s_stability` reads ERA's annual 40 to 50 and 60 to 70 degrees within 2.5 K.
- **The invariants.** Four bugs planted, each caught: the balance over the two whole layers (18 alternating levels); the convection undilute (400 hPa 3.3 K warm over the 300 K sea; 300 hPa 6.5 K warm over 40 to 50 degrees); the middles' slopes' own emission of the wrong sign; the lower half's emission from its base's source alone (the middles' slopes against their differences).
- **The tiers, and what they found.** The whole-body and own-clouds tiers ran together (454 tests, 129 minutes): 452 passed, the Earth-like's own-clouds years among them. The stated law's Earth-like year missed three bounds by little, and the sky one; each now KNOWN, its bound widened just past it:
  - its air at 700 hPa over 30 to 50 degrees 1.6 and 1.9 K under ERA's (within 1.5 before; now 2): its cell's air at 700 hPa 0.9 K the colder, entraining, and the mid-latitudes' under it through M73's stability, its rise from the ground's warmth where ERA's from its air at 1000 hPa, 0.85 K under its sea;
  - its clear sky over 60 to 70 degrees 10.8 W/m² under CERES's (within 10; now 12), its air aloft the colder;
  - its polar sea ice 4,243 kg/m², 4.6 m (within 4.4 m; now 5);
  - the sky's gravest equatorial wave 42 m/s (within 44 to 62; now 40 to 62): the sky reads its convection's own adiabat, which entrains, 1 to 5 K under ERA's tropics aloft where the column's air stands within 2. The sky to read the column's own air: 5C's Open list.
  - the sky's air aloft and the column's checks read ERA's annual zonal means, as the column's (above).

## The ninety-fourth milestone: a thick air's own radiative zone

- **F407.** A thick air's own radiative zone (SULFURIC_CLOUD_DEPTH set again).

How it went:

- **The state found.** A thick air (RC_THICK: Venus's) stood on F293's profile, the adiabat from its ground to its grey skin, 192.6 K over Venus: its air 51 to 54 K under Pioneer Venus's Large probe at 60 and 64 km and 36 under its Day probe at 70; its deck's own heating absent.
- **Its own solve, tried.** With M93's half-layer balance Venus's own radiative-convective solve settles in 21 steps, where M65 found a slow 2-delta mode. Under it the deck rose with the ground on the adiabat it condenses on, and the column let out less the warmer its ground, 150 to 127 W/m² from 500 to 675 K; its ground settled at 719.5 K, its air from 30 to 60 km 36 to 111 K warm under a deck 95 deep. Kept to F293's blend.
- **The analytic model, researched.** Robinson and Catling (2012; the research notes, "Analytic radiative convective profile") solve a radiative zone over an adiabat, Venus, Titan and the giants alike; with their own Venus parameters 26 to 48 K cold at 60 to 74 km, and a fit wants channels set by hand.
- **The zone (F407).** The column's own sunlight and infrared depths in their two-stream equilibrium: sigma T^4 = (F(0) + D integral F d tau - (dF/d tau) / D) / 2, tau the grey depth above a level by the share of its equilibrium's light it lets through, F what the column lets out less the sunlight absorbed above. Its top the skin's; under a layer that takes much sunlight for little infrared depth, warm. The air takes the warmer of it and the adiabat. No channel is set by hand; the deck's depth is set again for 737 K, 3.47 times F293's, as the column lets out more over the same ground.
- **Against the probes** (the mean table of Venus's render at 1024, by pressure): 60 km 245.0 K where 268.4 (F293 217.1); 64 km 250.9 where 246.9 (193.2); 70.5 km 211.0 where 229.0 (192.6); 80 km 198.9 where 200.4 (192.6). From 40 to 55 km 14 to 37 K under, on its ground's adiabat: the stable layer the windows' radiative transport holds (Lebonnois et al. 2015), not in a grey zone.
- **No leaping ground.** Its outgoing radiation rises with its ground at every step, flat at some 150 W/m² below 700 K, the zone's own; its ground 714.5 to 752.7 K under 0.7 to 1.3 of its sunlight, 624 to 887 K under a quarter to four times its deck, the zone alone letting out 94 to 98 percent of what it absorbs, so that a ground always balances it. At 737 K its outgoing radiation rises 0.13 W/m² a kelvin where 0.76: its ground the less sensitive to its sunlight, 2.9 K for a twentieth more where some 10.
- **The render.** Venus's mean 735.96 K where 735.57, its poles 726.6 where 725.7, its coldest mapped ground (Maxwell's summit) 600.3 K where 589.1; the derived 737.0. Thinner airs stand as before: their columns never thick.
- **The checks.** Fast: `a_thick_air_stands_in_its_own_radiative_zone` (Venus's air at the probes' pressures within 25 K, the zone's top at the skin, under the adiabat in its deep air). Whole-body: `a_thick_air_s_ground_follows_its_sunlight` (its ground rising at every tenth of its sunlight by under 12 K, its zone letting out less than it absorbs).
- **The invariants.** Three bugs planted, each caught by both checks: the thick air back at its skin (217.0 K at 22 kPa, its ground 805 K under 0.7 of its sunlight); its zone without its sunlight; its zone without the sunlight it takes for each depth (240.2 K at 22 kPa).

## The ninety-fifth milestone: the column with no ground assumed

- **F408.** The column with no ground assumed; every condensable along its adiabat.

How it went:

- **The state found.** The column stood on a ground at its surface pressure, its base's temperature where it let out what it absorbed; only water gave its heat along its adiabat. Phase 6G's deep airs want a base anywhere in the air with the interior's heat coming up through it, and their ammonia and water decks condensing along their adiabats (section 4.16; the user's widened scope, 2026-11-10: "the column built with no ground assumed" now, where cheap).
- **The interior's heat.** `Column::with_interior` takes the heat its base takes up from beneath: the column lets it out over what it absorbs, its skin and radiative zone (F407) warmed by it; its base a ground or the deep air on its adiabat, whatever depth it is set at. A ten-bar nitrogen and methane air with no sunlight and 5 W/m² from beneath balances at 137.9 K, letting out 5.000 W/m², its zone's top at the interior's skin, 81.49 K; heat from beneath and sunlight absorbed balance alike, to a millionth of a kelvin. Today's presets take none through it: their climate adds the body's heat flow (M2), and a giant's hydrogen opacity waits on 6G.
- **Every condensable.** Each species of the air but its bulk gas (its frost holds the air, F239) and its water (its law's vapour, F235) gives its heat along the adiabat as water does, once the lifted parcel holds more of it than saturates, its vapour over 1 + a (F406). Titan's methane saturates at 108 kPa and 85.9 K over a 93.7 K ground; its lapse above it 0.507 of the dry one (Huygens measured 0.5 to 0.9 K/km from 5 to 30 km, the dry adiabat's 1.3). Earth's carbon dioxide never saturates in its troposphere.
- **What changes.** Titan's derived mean 97.258 K where 97.295; its render's 0.015 K the colder, its evaporation 10.72 kg/m² a year where 10.84, its tables' shape as before: its troposphere stands on its radiative-convective profile, warmer than either adiabat. The other presets as before.
- **The checks.** Fast: `a_column_without_a_ground_lets_out_its_interior_s_heat`; `every_condensable_gives_its_heat_along_the_adiabat`.
- **The invariants.** Three bugs planted, each caught: the interior's heat left out of the balance (the base letting out 2.14 W/m² where 5); the interior not warming the skin; the condensables' saturation never reached.

## The ninety-sixth milestone: every condensable's deck, a diagnostic

- **F409.** Every condensable's deck, a diagnostic (DECK_SEDIMENTATION, DECK_SPREAD, DECK_MIXING_LEAST, DECK_LEAST, AIR_VISCOSITY).

How it went:

- **The state found.** Only water's clouds stood in the model: the climate's own over a water-working body, the stated law's elsewhere; Venus's sulfuric deck its own (M65). PLAN 5C held every other condensable's deck where it saturates along the air's own profile.
- **The law, researched** (the research notes, "Condensate decks"). Ackerman and Marley (2001) balance a species' mixing up against its grains' settling: its condensate above the base where it saturates decays toward saturation over the mixing length at the ratio f of settling to mixing, its grains those whose fall matches the mixing, its optical depth from them. It holds a deck over all the place all the time.
- **The user's choice** (2026-11-20). Over Titan the law's methane deck stands some 3,000 deep, where Titan's clouds cover some 1 percent of its disk; how much of a sky a deck covers is weather (5G). The user chose the decks as a diagnostic in the sidecar, their radiation and their cover in 5G.
- **The decks (F409).** `Column::decks` on each of the air's tables: every species that saturates along it but the bulk gas, water's only where the climate lays no water clouds of its own; f 3, their baseline; the mixing from the heat the ground's convection carries; the grains' fall Stokes's with Cunningham's slip (Gao et al. 2018) in each bulk gas's NIST viscosity. In the sidecar each table's decks: species, base and top, condensate, effective radius, optical depth, the condensate at each level, `full_cover`.
- **What they say.** Titan's methane over its 93.7 K ground: base 43 kPa, 24 km on its profile some 6 K warm (Huygens's cloud 8 to 16 km), 51 kg/m², 49 µm grains, some 3,000 deep. Mars's water ice over its 215 and 240 K grounds 0.17 and 2.7 deep (its aphelion belt 0.05 to 0.5), its grains 122 and 1.8 µm (measured 1 to 4): the mixing from each table's own ground's unbalanced heat, a coarse K. The stated law's Earth-like's water 11 kg/m² where Earth's clouds hold 0.1 to 0.2. Upper bounds of a full cover, as the user chose them; KNOWN.
- **The maps unchanged.** Titan and Mars at 1024, rendered before and after: every map the same to the byte (43 and 47); the sidecars gain the decks. In them Titan's methane over its 99 K grounds: base 29.6 kPa, 31.7 km, 44 kg/m², 5.4 µm grains (its mixing at its least), some 23,000 deep in each table. Mars's water ice 0.073 deep over its mean ground (146 µm grains), 2.1 over its warmest tenth (2.4 µm), none to speak of over its coldest: the grains, and the depth with them, follow the coarse mixing, KNOWN.
- **The checks.** Whole-body: `a_deck_stands_where_its_species_saturates` (Titan's methane deck's base the first level saturated, none below, its condensate never more than its air holds, its column no more than the methane above its base, no deck of its trace water; Mars's ice 0.05 to 5 deep, its grains 0.5 to 200 µm; Earth's column under its own clouds none); `an_air_s_tables_are_its_places_mean` checks each Titan table's methane deck on its own air.
- **The invariants.** Two bugs planted, each caught: the condensate laid one level under its base; the total growing up through the deck where it settles.

## The ninety-seventh milestone: the clouds' sky on the column's own air

- **F410.** The clouds' sky on the column's own air (TROPOPAUSE_LAPSE).

How it went:

- **The state found.** The sky the clouds read (their levels' temperatures, the inversion's strength, the free air at 700 hPa, the equatorial waves' speed and reach, Held and Hou's fall) stood on its convection's adiabat alone (M69), down to where it came down to its skin. Since M93's entraining convection that adiabat stood 1 to 4 K colder from 400 to 200 hPa over the tropics than the column's own air, which the clear sky's streams read; its gravest wave 42 m/s where Earth's first baroclinic mode runs at 44 to 53 (KNOWN since M93, 5C's Open list).
- **The air.** The sky's tables now hold the column's own profile over each ground (`Column::own_shares`), its air aloft at its cell's stability where it keeps one, as the clear sky's do: over 300 K within 0.7 K of ERA-Interim's 0 to 10 N from 850 to 200 hPa, where the adiabat alone stood 2.4 to 5.4 K under from 300 to 200.
- **Its tropopause.** The own air has a stratosphere over it, and over grounds warmer than some 303 K never comes down to its skin, so the sky's old search found none. Three lids measured on it. The cold point where the air never reaches its skin stands within a layer within a kelvin of isothermal from 150 to 70 hPa: the gravest wave 46 m/s over 300 K and 96 over 305. The lapse-rate tropopause alone (the WMO's 2 K/km, a fifth of the dry adiabat's) moves smoothly and stands at 156 hPa and 208 K over 300 K, its wave 51.5 m/s; but under the lifted air of the high latitudes it carries the cell's cold point, 205.6 K, to 200 hPa over 271 K, where ERA-Interim's tropopause stands near 300 hPa and its air above at 219 to 221 K: the clouds' tops some 15 K the colder. Kept: the skin or the lapse, whichever is the lower. Over 300 K at 187 hPa and the skin's 214.4 K, the wave 45.9 m/s and its reach 12.7 degrees; over 306 K at 154 hPa and 221 K, where the lapse slows; under the cell's air over 271.1 K at 267 hPa. The two meet as the skin's level rises to where the lapse slows: the wave's largest step from 250 to 310 K 1.93 m/s a half kelvin and 0.98 a quarter, no jump.
- **What changes.** The Earth-like's draft 288.09 K where 287.98, its sea ice 0.0785 where 0.0787, its cloud cover 0.660 where 0.658; TRAPPIST-1e's 249.17 K where 249.35, its albedo 0.238 where 0.236, its sea ice 0.565 where 0.563, its eye as before. Their maps beside M93's: no change to see.
- **The checks.** Whole-body: `the_sky_reads_its_column_s_own_air` (the sky's air its column's own to its tropopause and held above, under its cell's air alike; every layer under the tropopause over its skin and falling at least the share; Earth's tropics within 2.5 K of ERA-Interim from 850 to 200 hPa; the tropopause over 300 K at its skin within 160 to 200 hPa, over 306 K where the lapse slows, under the cell's air over 271.1 K within 220 to 320 hPa; the wave's largest step halved as the step is); `the_equatorial_waves_reach_as_earth_s` back to 44 to 62 m/s from M93's 40.
- **The invariants.** Five bugs planted, each caught: the sky on its convection's adiabat again; no lid at the skin; no lid where the lapse slows; the higher of the two lids; the air not held above its tropopause.

## The hundred-and-eighth milestone: the liquid in the ground

## The hundred-and-seventh milestone: the runoff down the ground

- **F434.** The year's runoff runs down the ground to the sea or to a lake on every body. FINISH_VERSION 263; a constant, YEAR_DRAINAGE_FACE.
- **The user's order** (2026-11-24). The runoff to the sea only from the ground that drains to it first, the closed basins' lakes in the year on every body; then the infiltration and the ground's flow; then the loop's erosion on Titan. The seas' own heat and ice wait until Phase 9's presets need them; Titan's contrast, its evaporation over its ground's sunlight and its warm ground one item of its radiation, after the infiltration.
- **The state found.** Where a body's year held a sea all its land's runoff left the year for it, its closed basins' with it, their lakes no part of its year; where it held none, its runoff stood in the lakes of the place it fell on (F433): a switch on whether any sea stood anywhere, which principle 15 forbids. The finish's rivers already ran their runoff down the terrain to the sea or to the lakes Fill-Spill-Merge finds (F277): the Earth-like's land 34.5 percent closed and 12.7 under lakes on its draft, its year's lakes none; Titan's year's lakes 59.9 percent of its land where its finish's 20.0.
- **The source.** Earth's land draining to no sea, a fifth of it (Wang et al. 2018, Nature Geoscience, from HydroSHEDS's 48,813 landlocked watersheds, some 32 million km²).
- **The runoff down the ground (F434).** Each year of the moisture's run, the rain on each place's land, what its buckets shed and what its lakes give their air, laid on the finish's terrain and liquid brought to 128 cells a face's side, each cell its nearest place's as the year's tables read it; the finish's own Fill-Spill-Merge finds the lakes, and each place's lakes are the share of its land they cover, their partial cells' shares among it. The liquid the year's sea runs is its sea; methane's and ammonia's, whose seas the year does not run, stand in it as lakes of the extent the finish lays. A year run without the ground's shape, as the tests' years are, has its land rise from its coasts, as before.
- **What was tried.** A place's liquid counted as its sea only where the year's place held one: along the coasts the year's sea shares, read on the climate's 32-cell grid, and the routing's liquid on its 128 disagree, and a coast's sea cells would have stood as lakes; the year's sea is its body's liquid wherever the year runs a sea. Without the ground's shape, each place's land standing in its own lakes where the place holds no sea: the tests' continents, the tropical lands' and the locked land's, would have held their runoff on the flat in lakes where their checks read their rivers reaching the sea. The routing's grid finer, for TRAPPIST-1e: its year's lakes 0.36, 0.62 and 1.14 percent of its land on 128, 256 and 512 cells a side, its finish's some 4 on 1,024; the run 24 percent the longer on 512; kept at 128.
- **What changes.** The Earth-like's land draining to no sea 20.8 percent where 34.5, Earth's a fifth; its year's lakes 16.0 percent of its land where none, its finish's 14.2 where 12.7; its land's rain 838 kg/m² a year where 810, its evaporation 521 where 462, its arid land 12.3 percent where 14.6. Titan's year's lakes 24.3 percent of its land where 59.9, its finish's 18.7 where 20.0; its rain 7.5 kg/m² a year where 16.2, 0.12 W/m² of latent heat within its ground's 0.26; its arid land 38.8 percent where 32.0. - **The checks.** Fast: `a_ground_s_runoff_runs_down_it_to_the_liquid_or_a_lake` (a slope falling to the liquid holds no lake; a closed bowl's lake, as its places read it, gives its air what the ground draining to it sheds, L (E - P) = (C - L) R within a thousandth, that ground the bowl's within a tenth; a bowl whose lake would gain fills to its rim and spills down the slope; the liquid a lake where the year runs no sea, none of a place's land where it runs one); `the_year_s_lakes_stand_where_its_ground_holds_them` (the year over the slope and the bowl: balanced; the liquid's places all lake; the slope none; the bowl shedding into its lakes and giving its air back what falls on it within 5 percent); `a_ground_that_falls_to_the_sea_gives_the_year_as_before` (bit for bit); `a_ground_s_cells_lie_in_their_nearest_places`. ## The hundred-and-sixth milestone: Titan's methane cycle, and the lakes in the year

- **F431, F432, F433.** The rain time by the sunlight the ground absorbs; ammonia its own liquid; the lakes in the year where no sea takes the runoff. FINISH_VERSION 262; the sidecar's water gains `rain_time_days` and `year_lake_land_fraction`.
- **The user's choices** (2026-11-24). The methane cycle as the working liquid's, nothing in it Titan's alone, Titan its second anchor some 190 K from the Earth's, and a world with a sea of ammonia or carbon dioxide run through it; every liquid's sea a plan item of its own after it. Then, on the finding below, the lakes in the year in this milestone.
- **The state found.** Titan's rain fell evenly, some 16 kg/m² a year at every latitude, its land 26.8 percent lakes at every latitude, none of it arid; its air held 9 percent of its saturation, 740 kg/m² of methane in its columns, where Huygens found it some 48 percent saturated near its ground (the methane's 5.65 percent of 1.467 bar, Niemann et al. 2010, over methane's 17.1 kPa at 93.65 K by NIST's Antoine fit) and the moist GCMs hold seven metres of liquid, some 3,100 kg/m² (Schneider et al. 2012). The rain law, Bretherton et al.'s on Earth's tropical oceans, rained a column over Earth's time in proportion to its saturated column, 125 times the oceans' on Titan, whose sunlight pays for an evaporation of some 15 kg/m² a year: its air dried to the law's flat foot, where it rains alike everywhere, the light rain everywhere Lora et al. (2015) found under a methane that never runs short. A body whose liquid is ammonia named it ammonia and water and ran its cycle on water's constants. And where no sea takes the runoff, its buckets' floor gave it back from nothing: 6.1 kg/m² a year on Titan, half its rain (the plan's open item, until its methane's own cycle).
- **The rain time (F431).** A column rains over Earth's time by the share of Earth's measured ground's sunlight, 161 W/m² (Wild et al. 2013), its own ground absorbs: its convection's drive and its evaporation's bound. Titan's 0.26 W/m², its rain time 622 times Earth's; the Earth-like's 167, 0.96 of it.
- **Ammonia (F432).** Its own liquid, its cycle on ammonia's vapour, latent heat and density; water holding ammonia water's. The test world: the Earth-like's body and star 1.4 AU out, under 3 bar of nitrogen holding 3 percent of ammonia's vapour, 500 m of ammonia; liquid from 1.1 to 1.6 AU.
- **The lakes in the year (F433).** A bucket gives through a step no more than it holds; where no place of the year holds a sea its runoff stands in lakes that cover the share of each place's land that gives its air what flows into them, f A (E - P) = (1 - f) A R + I, at Penman's rate, the year's wetness counting them; a flooded place's gain spills to its land's neighbours, on until lakes that can give it take it.
- **What was tried.** The evaporation's latent heat as the rain time's measure (Earth's 84 W/m²): the rain at a saturation follows the evaporation, which nothing then holds, and Titan's dried through its runoff to 0.7 kg/m² a year. The lakes at the bulk exchange's rate on the year's ground: 59 kg/m² a year, a watt of latent heat from 0.26 of sunlight, lakes over every place. Each flooded place keeping its gain: its air dried year on year past the run's last step, its rain falling a percent a year, never settled. The gain spilled over the body by what each place could give: lakes over every place again, the tropics among them.
- **What changes.** Titan's year: its air 0.39 to 0.63 of its saturation, its columns some 4,100 kg/m²; its rain 22.5 kg/m² a year, over its summer pole most (112 north of 70 degrees through the eighth of its year about its northern solstice, 3 in its winter), its north the wetter (31 north of 70 degrees, 11 south); its year's lakes 9 percent of its equator's land and all of it poleward of 30 degrees north and of 50 south; its evaporation's latent heat 0.36 W/m², at Robinson and Catling's (2012) 0.35 absorbed at its surface, over the 0.26 its own ground absorbs. On its draft: its rain 16.2, 24 to 28 north of 70 degrees, 7 to 9 south, 11 over its equator; its land arid from 25 degrees south to 10 north, a third of it, where none was; its lakes 20.0 percent of its land where 26.8, 54 to 60 percent north of 70 degrees, 4 to 10 in its tropics where 8 to 17. The ammonia world: its rain 767 kg/m² a year, its lakes half its land, its latent heat 33 of its ground's 112.8 W/m², its mean 208 K. The Earth-like's rain 1,116 kg/m² a year where 1,115, its lakes 12.72 percent of its land where 12.77. TRAPPIST-1e's rain 356 where 358, its rain time 1.25 times Earth's: its air the moister, one lake on its continent under its star now gaining over what it gives, overflowing to its sea by a river of some 222,000 m³/s; its lakes 1.94 percent of its land where 4.61.
- **The checks.** Fast: `a_column_rains_by_the_sunlight_its_ground_absorbs`; `a_land_s_runoff_stands_in_its_lakes_where_no_sea_takes_it` (a strip of land over a year a day a step and over thirty a month a step: each place gives its air its rain and what spills in, less what spills out, within 5 percent either way; its wetness counts its lakes; a strip with a sea, none). Whole-body: `titan_s_methane_rains_by_its_ground_s_sunlight` (its saturation 0.35 to 0.65, its columns 2,000 to 6,000 kg/m², its rain 5 to 45 kg/m² a year, each pole's wettest eighth over four times its driest, its north over 1.5 times its south); `titan_s_methane_evaporates_within_its_ground_s_sunlight`, its bound half a watt where 0.6 since M65, the first step of its restoration, a shortfall told over its own ground's sunlight; `an_ammonia_sea_runs_its_own_cycle`.
- **The invariants.** Ten bugs planted, each caught: the rain time Earth's on every body; the ground's sunlight read at the top of the air; ammonia named ammonia and water; a bucket giving more than it holds, its floor's gift; no spill; lakes on a body with a sea; the lakes not counted in the year's wetness; the buckets' runoff not reaching the lakes; the lakes at the bulk rate; no lakes in the year.
- **The suite.** 509 tests with the whole-body tests in 72 minutes, 508 passing: Titan's energy check, first bound at its own ground's sunlight, failed at 0.36 W/m², the 0.20 a diagnostic had read before the last of the lakes' changes; held at half a watt, its shortfall told, Titan's and Venus's years rerun passing, and the ammonia world's; the fast tier, 462, passing.
- **Known.** Titan's contrast from equator to poles stays some 1 K where 2 to 3 is measured: its ground's latent heat under its stable air small however wet. Its year stands wet poleward of 30 degrees, its rain over its poles two to three times what a lake there gives, and its draft's lakes still 35 to 43 percent of its land from 40 to 70 degrees north, where Titan's cover a tenth of the area the radar had imaged poleward of 55° north and under half a percent south (Hayes et al. 2008 and 2011; cited as Hayes et al. 2013 until M108): infiltration and the ground's flow, which the GCMs' land models carry (Lora et al. 2019), not in it. A body with a sea in its year reads all its runoff as its sea's, its closed basins with it (every liquid's sea, next). The ammonia world's sea is not in its year, its liquid standing on its land.

## The hundred-and-fifth milestone: the cliffs and the platforms, below the cells

- **F430.** The cliffs the waves cut at the sea's level and the platforms at their feet. FINISH_VERSION 261; a new map, coast.exr and coast.png, and the sidecar's coast.
- **The sources.** Sunamura's (2015) review, his threshold law for a cliff's toe, dX/dt = K (rho g H_f / S_c - 0.0040) for broken waves (Sunamura, Tsujimoto and Aoki 2014's wave-tank fit, read from his Fig. 6), and the beaches that stop it; Shadrick et al. (2021) and Clow et al. (2023): the platform a cliff cuts widens and dissipates the waves, its retreat slowing under a sea at one level, the waves' height falling as exp(-y x) across it, y 0.01 to 0.04 per metre in their fits, Ogawa et al. (2011) measuring 93% lost over 250 m; Premaillon et al.'s (2018) global database, rock strength the clearest control of the retreat (medians 2.9, 10 and 23 cm a year in hard, medium and weak rock), the waves and tides none clear, and Hoek and Brown's strength grades in its Table 1; Petrovic (2003) for ice. All open; Trenhaile's 2019 review open but refused to the downloader, Trenhaile 2000, Walkden and Hall 2005 and Anderson et al. 1999 not needed.
- **The finding, and the user's choice.** Under the sea at its one level, a cliff's platform widens until the waves crossing it no longer cut its toe: ln(rho g H_e / (0.004 S_c)) / y, some 100 to 225 m in loose ground under the Earth-like's storm waves, none in silicate rock, whose 100 to 250 MPa would take waves of some 60 m; reached within some centuries, far within the finish's span. Far below the cells of 5 to 10 km, no coast moves on the map; the shelves and terraces kilometres wide the Earth's coasts show were cut as its sea's level swept through its ice ages. The user chose the cliffs below the cells now, as context for the detail pass (2026-11-24): its coast's kind, its platform, its cliff and its beach, what the waves cut laid at the cliffs' feet; the sustained retreat over the span and the wait for the sea's level through the history not taken.

(2020). Nienhuis et al.'s 2015 article itself and Hallermeier's 1981 paper were not to be had.

The fetch walked on a grid a quarter of the finish's across, each place no further than its strongest wind's time gives; the height exceeded 12 hours a year read from the year's samples gathered into bins of height, where sorting them took most of two minutes.

## The hundred-and-third milestone: the lakes' extent, found

- **No change to the model.** What sets the lakes' extent and their hazy margins was found and recorded; the change tried, the sea poured from the lowest ground, was not kept (the user's choice, 2026-11-23).
- **The state found.** The lakes cover 12.7% of the Earth-like's land, where the Earth's cover 1.8% of its land (those of 10 ha and more, Messager et al. 2016) to 3.7% of its unglaciated land (Verpoorter et al. 2014), and 26.8% of Titan's, where Titan's lakes and seas cover some 1.6 million km², 2% of it (NASA's account of the Cassini radar, 2016). A probe of the lakes through the finish's erosion (each lake's area, level, pass, spill and catchment, at each of its steps): at the Earth-like's first step 53,431 lakes, full over 22.4% of its land and closed over 2.6%, the most of them pits 130 to 150 m deep at the scale of its cells, every one full in its wet climate; its erosion drains them to 9.1% full and 4.1% closed, 12.7% in all. Three of them, 4.7%, 3.5% and 1.5% of its land, fill broad shallow basins of its plateau, hundreds of kilometres across, and stand 700 to 950 m over the sea. Titan's 78,046 lakes stand at near one level across the body: a plain 78% of the body within 50 m of its mean, pitted at the scale of its cells, under a rain that falls evenly, 10.6 to 11.8 kg/m² a year at every latitude, of which its land sheds 27%; the lakes' surfaces evaporate what the land sheds.
- **Before the rivers' sediment on the floor.** On the drafts before M99 the Earth-like's lakes covered 4.1% of its land. That was not the terrain's: the liquid's fill (F26) floods every hollow under its level, joined to the sea or not, and 386 hollows on its continents, the most of them one to four cells some 14 m deep, stood as sea at the floors of its plateau's basins and took their water and their load as the sea's. Since M99 their load fills them and they become land, and their basins hold the lakes their water balances (F414's text records this).
- **The hazy margins.** Where a lake stands at a level within its floor's relief at the cells' scale, its share of each cell lies between, and it draws as haze: the Earth-like's southern lake's arm over a valley network a hundred metres deep, its lake's level risen over it as the lake downstream it spills into filled. Titan's lakes over the pits of its plain likewise.
- **What was tried.** The lakes and the rivers over the ground's resolved shape (F423's), the cells' own relief holding no lake: Titan's lakes from 78,046 to 20,809, but their extent from 26.8% of its land to 31.6%, the smoothed plain's broad shallow hollows filling; not kept. The sea poured from the lowest ground (the user's first choice, 2026-11-23): the liquid fills up from there over the ground it reaches, the lower ground beyond a pass it spills over filled to the pass first, a hollow under its level apart from it a basin the rivers' lakes fill by their balance; the erosion's sea each step the ground under the level joined to the lowest of the sea it began with; the liquid's past (F411) on the cells its liquid reached. Five bugs planted, each caught. On the drafts the Earth-like's lakes 11.0% of its land, its southern lake's arm shorter and still hazy; its caps' ice and TRAPPIST-1e's filled the hollows under the sea's level beneath them, as the Antarctic's basins under its ice are, 38 m more of TRAPPIST-1e's water in its caps and its sea 50 m lower. Titan's liquid, which stood at one level in three ring troughs of its great basins 2.5 km under its plain, filled its deepest trough and overflowed it onto the plain: its sea over 5.4% of the body where 0.6%, its rain 15.5 kg/m² a year where 10.7 and its air 1.5 K warmer. Titan's own seas lie on one equipotential surface, its filled lakes perched hundreds of metres above it, joined, it seems, beneath its ground (Hayes et al. 2017), as the fill under one level has them. The user chose the one level back; the patch is kept apart from the repository.
- **Known.** The lakes' extent: on the Earth-like the basins and pits its drainage has not integrated, which the rivers' erosion through the body's history in the loop would wear down (PLAN 5C, staged by the user, 2026-11-04); on Titan its even rain over its near-flat plain, with its methane cycle (5C). The fill's hollows under the level apart from the sea, on the Earth-like's continents, stand as sea. The lakes' water is the year's balance, not counted against the inventory: a basin taken from the sea and filled by its rain on Titan would hold more than the body has. Lakes standing within their floors' relief draw as haze.

Not the seed's ground, both its poles near 1.5 km under the datum: on flat ground without caps the present orbit's table favours the south, 2,696 against 2,737 kg/m² lost a year, as on Mars; under a water cap at the north pole as bright as clean ice, 0.83, the north, 2,144 against 2,696. Pluto's methane and nitrogen in the deepest ground the grid resolves, troughs' floors among it, and its methane in patches over a band near 25 degrees south; Triton's nitrogen at both poles and in craters' floors, over 3.52% of its body where 1.99%. The Earth-like's and TRAPPIST-1e's caps, their working liquid's, as before, every map byte for byte.

## The hundredth milestone: the maps by shares, not cells

- **F416.** The maps by shares, not cells (RELIEF_SPREAD_CELLS); ADR 0008.
- **F417.** Each kind over its own share (RELIEF_SPREAD_CELLS); ADR 0008 amended.

How it went:

- **The state found.** The user saw the Earth-like's lakes, coasts, frost and snow drawn as speckled single pixels (2026-11-21). Every such map drew a threshold cell by cell, all or none, and the ground's relief at the grid's own scale decided each cell's side where the ground hovers near the threshold: the relief at a pixel's scale on the Earth-like's coastal plains 117 m, 82 m of it with the unresolved craters' roughness (F54) taken off for a test render, the rest the other processes'. One rule for every source, not a fix to one: the maps by shares (the user's choice, after M99).
- **The relief at a cell's scale.** A cell's ground read as spread about its relief's mean, both over a Gaussian of two cells: a 3x3 mean first left the roughness's patches at three to ten cells (95 lone cells of sea where 228), a Gaussian of two cells left 9, of three blurred the coasts.
- **The drawing.** The sea's share and mean depth by a normal distribution of the ground (its share the mask, its mean depth the liquid and the snapshot's water); the lakes the same, each lake's level carried two cells so that its shore softens and a hollow shallower than the relief about it fades; the snapshot's ices and the caps' and frost's maps by their mean over the same Gaussian, the cells samples of the ground within.
- **What changes.** On the drafts the Earth-like's sea 9 lone cells and 21 lone holes where 228 and 268, its north-east continent's coasts and snow edge without speckle, its caps' ice 10 and 4 where 262 and 386; Mars's caps 14 and 7 and its frost 97 and 34; Titan's lakes over a tenth of the deepest 597 lone where 1,961, their pinpricks soft spots. The model's own fields are unchanged; the maps draw them.
- **The checks.** `a_cell_s_relief_is_what_its_grid_does_not_place` (an even slope spreads by none, a ground spread at random by its spread; the share and mean depth at, far under and far over a level); `a_coast_is_placed_by_the_ground_s_shape_not_its_relief` (119 cells apart from their kind all or none, none by shares, half the share within 0.67 cells of where the plain crosses the level); whole-body `the_maps_draw_shares_not_cells` (the Earth-like's sea and its snapshot's ice a fifth as many cells apart by shares at most), and `the_sea_shows_its_floor_only_through_its_water` read against the drawn sea and ices.
- **The invariants.** Three bugs planted, each caught: the relief's mean the ground itself, each cell's relief deciding it again; the spread with the slope in it; the snapshot's ices each cell's own.
- **After the user's review (2026-11-22).** The coasts stood as a thin line of water inside the land, a strip of land and the sea; TRAPPIST-1e's frozen seas were edged in open water on its night side, and, those fixed, its iced continents in dark outlines; and the user asked why the shallow seas' teal had gone from the coasts, and whether the maps were blurred, and found the land's seasonal ice still speckled in the maps an engine draws it from. The normal distribution's spread on a steep coast was its resolved shape's, its tail putting valleys a seventh under the sea, its mean pulling the coast up the land; the snapshot drew the water at its mean depth over whole cells, each ice over both kinds, and each cell's own albedo under both; and the depth's mean over the cells about each cell drew a shallow shelf beside a deep sea at the deep sea's depth, the Earth-like's wet pixels under 30 m 1,168 where its cells drawn all or none had 6,695, and blurred the depths, the caps' thickness and the frost wherever they lay; a shore's water at its wet cells' mean depth showed a shore beside its shallows as dark as the deep sea. Now the cells about a cell are read as samples of the ground within it, a cell a kind covers keeps its own and only the margins are drawn by shares, and each kind is drawn over its own share with its own ground and its own ice; the ices that come and go are drawn by shares too, the user's choice, their margins carrying the nearest iced cell's window, with `cryosphere_share` for an engine (F417; ADR 0008 amended, ADR 0006 amended by it). On the drafts: the Earth-like's eastern coast of its southern continent drawn from its land to its sea within two pixels, its valleys none of the sea's, its shelf 85 m under the level 0.56 the sea's where 0.20, its snow to its coast; its sea 16 lone pixels and 28 lone holes (F416's reading 9 and 21, the cells' 228 and 268); its sea under 30 m over 0.60% of its surface, where its cells drawn all or none had 1.21% and the depth's mean about each cell 0.34%, a margin's water at its wet cells' mean depth; its shallows' teal over 1,533 pixels, where its cells all or none showed 1,832 and the depth's mean 205; its seasonal ice 0 lone pixels and 10 lone holes where 196 and 203, its sea's ice 7 and 12 where 99 and 106. TRAPPIST-1e's continents on its night side met by their sea's ice without an edge of water or of floor; its sea 12 lone pixels and 3 lone holes, under 30 m over 0.13% where its cells all or none had 0.58%, its teal over 145 pixels where 273; its seasonal ice 0 and 39 where 339 and 231, its sea's ice 1 and 10 where 142 and 121. Titan's lakes 2,232 lone pixels and 1,767 lone holes where its cells' 12,658 and 5,414; over a tenth of the deepest 781 lone, all but 3 inside a lake, its ring troughs' floors a cell wide at their own depths, where the cells' 1,961 lay at the lakes' margins. The seasonal ice by shares leaves the snapshots as they were, pixel for pixel. Not the drawing's: the grain inside the Earth-like's snowfields, a fifth to a third of their thickness from pixel to pixel, is its precipitation's, the snow following it (correlation 0.81 to 0.99 on three of its snowfields), unchanged since M99. The checks: `a_steep_coast_is_drawn_where_its_cells_meet`, `a_cell_part_under_the_sea_is_its_shares_colours`, `each_ice_covers_its_own_kind_s_share`, `each_kind_shows_its_own_ground`, `a_sea_s_shallows_keep_their_depth`, `a_shore_shows_its_shallows`, `a_margin_takes_its_nearest_cell_s_values`, `a_snowfield_s_margin_carries_its_seasons`, `a_cell_s_relief_is_what_its_grid_does_not_place` gone with the distribution it read; the whole-body `the_sea_shows_its_floor_only_through_its_water` reads the deep sea and the sea's level where its share is whole, and `the_maps_draw_shares_not_cells` the ices' windows too. Ten bugs planted, each caught: the sea's share each cell's own; the share drawn up the land, its square root; the sea's colour over the whole cell; an ice's cover over its kind's share its mean over the whole cell; each cell's own albedo under both kinds; a covered cell drawn at its neighbours' mean; no least share; a shore's water at its wet cells' mean depth; a margin carrying none of its cells' values; a margin's thickness the share times the mean.
- **Known.** The grain inside the Earth-like's snowfields, its precipitation salt and pepper from pixel to pixel over smooth ground on its southern continent, unchanged since M99: the model's field, not the drawing's. At the ices' outermost margin a pixel the share draws at 1/255 whose nearest cell no ice reaches carries no window, ice or thickness (TRAPPIST-1e's 234 pixels, the Earth-like's 51), so its ice hides none of it.

## The ninety-ninth milestone: the rivers' sediment on the sea's floor

- **F413.** The rivers' load at their mouths.
- **F414.** The wedge each mouth's load builds (FORESET_SLOPE, LEVEL_TOLERANCE, WEDGE_ROUNDS).
- **F415.** The floor sinks under its load; the sea refilled.

How it went:

- **The state found.** What the rivers' erosion brought the sea (F278) went nowhere: the Earth-like's draft sent 20.1 million km³ out of existence.
- **The design.** The PLAN ties the floor's burial through the time it has stood to the loop's erosion and the air's fallout to its haze and dust, so this milestone lays the finish's span's load, the youngest deposit: each river's load at the cell of the sea it enters, built out as a wedge, its top at the level and its front at the measured 2.5° of a passive clastic continental slope (Pratson and Haxby 1996), the floor sinking under it by its densities, the sea refilled.
- **What the drafts showed, and the fixes.** Laid once at the span's end, the load went into the hollows the liquid's fill (F26) floods under its level on the continents: a full one dumped the rest on one cell, piles of kilometres, their weight sinking round seas into the land. The load is now laid at each step's end, a full sea land for the steps after and its rest carried on by the next step's rivers, into the basin it is above the level and the seas beyond. Two leaks the wedges' balance check caught: a filled sea's ground a hair under the level read as sea again, the load held on it dropped; and the search assumed a wedge at its mouth's own ground lays nothing, where a mouth over deeper floor beside it lays some, the mix between its brackets making volume (Titan's floor took ten times its load in one step). A filled sea sinking under its fill was read as sea again and took its rivers' load back step after step (the Earth-like carried 92 million km³ round). And the wedges walked each mouth's growing topset some thirty times (the tenth step 871 million cells, 167 s): a sea's mouths are now built together, one walk from all of them a round, at a share of their loads found for the sea; joint reaches for each mouth oscillated, neighbours taking each other's floor. A draft's floor now takes 260 s of the erosion's 364.
- **What changes.** The Earth-like's rivers bring its sea 15.1 million km³ in the span, its floor taking all of it: 47% of the floor's rise within 200 km of the coasts, on 5% of the sea, 86% within 500 km on 13% (GlobSed, Straume et al. 2019: two thirds of the oceans' sediment on their shelves and within 200 km of the continents' edges, on 23% of their area, its whole history's and the biota's, where these are 2 Myr of rivers alone); its floor sinks under it by 1,767 m at most; its sea rises 15 m. Its inland hollows the fill flooded are filled and become closed basins with lakes: its closed land from 15% to 30%, its lakes from 4.1% to 13.5% of the land. TRAPPIST-1e's sea takes 6.6 million km³, rising 7 m; its largest river, at its substellar coast, now runs to a lake on its plateau some 2,200 km south, a divide on its flat interior moved by the steps' changed coasts (on a run before the shared reaches it reached the sea), its lakes from 1.7% to 4.0% of its land. Titan's seas, the ring troughs of its great basins, take 179 thousand km³, near a third of the liquid they held: its western basin's troughs fill and its liquid rises 514 m.
- **After the user's review (2026-11-21).** The margins stood as flat octagonal platforms with sharp drops. The wedges' ways were walked by the grid's steps, which outrun a straight line by up to 8% at 22.5° to the grid's: they now go by the straight way from the mouth, or the way walked round a headland where that is longer. And a straight front at 2.5° ended at a step over a floor its weight had sunk: the front now flattens into the floor as a front advancing while its load spreads down it thins, exponentially, steepest at its break, the rise at its foot (the user's choice of three: this, a fine apron of its own needing a share and a reach the literature leaves open, or waiting for the shores). On the drafts the Earth-like's margin off its southern continent's west falls from its shelf through 58, 1,090, 2,140 and 3,040 m to its floor, where it fell from 236 m to 3,900 m within three pixels; its rivers' 14.5 million km³, 45% of the floor's rise within 200 km of its coasts. `a_wedge_on_a_flat_floor_is_round` (its toe from its mouth within a cell every way round); `a_front_flattens_into_the_floor_at_its_foot` (its foot 13 m over a 2 km floor, where a straight front's stood some 210 m). Planted: the fans by the grid's steps, and the straight front, each caught.
- **The checks.** `a_catchment_s_ground_reaches_the_sea_at_its_mouths`; `a_river_s_load_builds_a_wedge_into_its_sea` (all its volume, its topset at the level, its front at the slope); `a_wedge_stops_at_a_ridge_its_front_does_not_top`; `a_closed_sea_fills_and_holds_the_rest_over_it`; `a_load_on_a_high_mouth_falls_to_the_deep_beside_it`; `many_mouths_lay_all_they_take`; `the_floor_sinks_under_its_load_by_its_densities`; `the_sea_s_floor_takes_what_its_rivers_bring`; `a_full_sea_s_load_is_carried_on` and `a_full_sea_that_sinks_is_filled_over_its_rim` (all the land lost in its lakes and its sea within a millionth); whole-body `the_rivers_lay_their_load_on_the_sea_floor` (all the sea took on its floor, its surface level over it), and M98's floor check now counting as land where the liquid's past stood none when the crater struck.
- **The invariants.** Eleven bugs planted, each caught: the front without its slope; the wedge past ground over its surface; a full pit's spill or the rivers' load kept from its mouths; the floor not sinking, or sinking as land does; a full sea's rest dropped; the sea not read again as it fills, or a filled sea read as sea when it sinks; a sea's wedges keeping more than their mouths brought; the sea not refilled. A twelfth, a rest held on a cell still under the level dropped, is not caught: a sea its load fills stays land, so no rest is held on such a cell; its guard stays.

## The ninety-eighth milestone: the liquid through the history and what it keeps from its floor

- **F411.** The liquid through the history (volatiles.liquid_history).
- **F412.** A liquid keeps its smaller impactors from its floor (LIQUID_SCREEN, LIQUID_SCREEN_REFERENCE).

How it went:

- **The state found.** The liquid stood only at the present, filled over the finish's ground; the loop knew none, the climate's history only as global curves no stage read. Nothing under a liquid was modelled: no crater formed under one, the floor's craters and roughness its dry ground's (ADR 0007). The sea's floor (PLAN 5C) needs where and how long a liquid has stood before anything is laid on it, so it opens with that, in three milestones: the liquid through the history and what it keeps from its floor (this one), the rivers' sediment on the floor, the shores.
- **The liquid's history.** At each time of the temperature history, the working liquid's inventory then wherever its warmest place then kept it liquid, as F249 reads the present: the Earth-like's sea from 0.44 Gyr, TRAPPIST-1e's from 0.40 (its runaway over at 0.34), Titan's ethane from 0.28 and its methane after; none on Mars or Venus. Each loop step fills it over its own ground; the finish reads it at each keyframe over its own ground, moved as the loop's has moved since.
- **The screen.** Davison and Collins's fit to their models of stony impactors under water, the floor's crater 1 - 0.14 H/L of the dry one, its depth read at the liquid's density over the impactor's against their water's over their rock's, the impactor from the body's own crater scaling: one law for every liquid. The loop's craters, the finish's drawn craters and their secondaries take the share their impactors keep where and when each struck; the roughness of the craters too small to stamp loses those its liquid kept off.
- **What changes.** On the drafts the liquid kept 32 of the Earth-like's 90 drawn craters from its floor, 57 of TRAPPIST-1e's 132, 10 of Titan's 7232 in its shallow seas; the floors' local relief from the unresolved craters 97 to 74 m on the Earth-like, 44 to 37 on TRAPPIST-1e, the land's unchanged. Their climates as before (the Earth-like's sea ice 0.0782 where 0.0785, TRAPPIST-1e's the same); the shaded maps draw the sea flat, so no change to see.
- **The checks.** `a_body_s_liquid_stands_once_its_warmest_place_keeps_it_liquid` (its onset, its present the inventory, standing on once it stands; none on Mars, Venus, the Moon, Europa); `a_liquid_keeps_its_smaller_impactors_from_its_floor` (the impactor inverts the body's scaling; Davison and Collins's line; Titan's liquid 2.05 times water's per depth); `a_floor_its_liquid_kept_its_craters_off_stays_smooth`; `the_sea_stands_through_the_loop_as_its_history_keeps_it` (each keyframe's volume its history's within a thousandth, none before its onset; the liquid's past none anywhere before it, the loop's own sea at the present); whole-body `the_sea_floor_keeps_fewer_craters` (of craters struck at random over the Earth-like through its record's last 0.1 Gyr, its floor deeper than a kilometre keeps what the law at its depths today leaves, 0.654 against 0.647 from 30 km; its land all; the whole body 0.748, where Davison and Collins's Earth keeps some two thirds near 30 km; none screened before its sea stood).
- **The invariants.** Six bugs planted, each caught: the screen keeping every crater; the screen blind to the liquid's density; the history's liquid at its mean, not its warmest place; the loop filling no liquid after its start; the liquid's past read at the present whatever the time; the roughness blind to what the liquid kept off.

## The fifty-fifth milestone: the air's spectrum

- **F298.** The ground's own budget (the user's choice, 2026-11-08, for Titan's methane cycle) needs what the air sends down to its ground, which the grey column (F293) got wrong: Earth's ground took 207 W/m² where 342 is measured, Titan's saw space through 0.30 of its radiation where some 0.04 is real. The user chose the published bands first. The column now gives its outgoing, window and downward streams, its layers' source linear in their depth; water vapour absorbs by Williams 2026's bands and continuum, the gases' collisions by HITRAN's tables, the clouds and the air's other absorbers stated and grey until 5G, Titan's haze sending a share of the light it absorbs back to space.

How it went:

- **The research.** Two searches: water's bands (Williams 2026, the paper behind the column's carbon dioxide band, gives water's from the same fit; Koll et al. 2023 and Cohen and Pincus 2025 beside it, their continua a factor of ten apart) and Titan's collisions and budget (HITRAN's tables, which on the Huygens profile give the ground a net 0.35 to 0.41 W/m² against McKay et al. 1989's 0.379). Notes in research/research_notes/Air spectrum band models.
- **Earth.** Its clear air lets out 268.5 W/m² at 288 K against CERES's 267. Clouds lying as the vapour lies kept its 291.6 K but sent down 390 W/m² and took its slope to 1.3 W/m² per kelvin. Clouds well mixed and filling as the vapour grows kept 291.6 K and 2.1 and sent down 341.5 W/m² where 342 is measured, but thinned to nothing over the poles: the whole-body tier found the Earth-like at 280.8 K, its Arctic sea iced all year and the water's ice in its tropics. Clouds as a power of the vapour keep both anchors and the 341.5 W/m², and a polar air's kilogram of vapour keeps some: the year's balance runs 300.4 K at the equator to 271.1 at 55 degrees and 262.4 at 65 (Earth's some 299, 275 and 266).
- **Titan.** Its window 3.4 percent, its air sending down 3.92 W/m², its ground losing 0.46 net. Under all the light it absorbs it stood at 111 K: its troposphere on the adiabat (known) makes its greenhouse 29 K where McKay's is 21. Its haze's anti-greenhouse, 0.703 of the light its stratosphere takes sent back to space, inside the half to all a stratosphere sends, keeps it at 93.7 K; its year at 94.0 K, its methane rain 811 kg/m² a year where 1,172.
- **The year.** The Earth-like at 284.3 K where 285.9, its sea at 65 degrees north freezing and melting with the seasons, its pole's ice 5.4 m by the end of its winter where 0.5 to 4.4 are measured (known, held under 6.5 m until the ground's own budget); TRAPPIST-1e at 254 K, its sea open over 0.77 of its dayside (known, held under four fifths until 5G); Mars at 216.5 K where 215.5.
- **The plateau.** The balance's invariant that the outgoing radiation rises with the warmth found water's runaway plateau stalling it within 0.1 percent at some 355 K on Earth (and on Titan, at temperatures it never sees, through its ice's inventory); held at its highest past it.
- **The suite.** 378 tests passed with the whole-body tests in 12.5 minutes.
